Starting a business in Cyprus, from the barest registration to a café

What it takes, in steps, fees and published times, to trade in Cyprus in one's own name, as a company, online and with premises; what each step up gives in return; how businesses opened in 2022–2026 went through it; whom to hire for which step and what firms publish as their prices; what changes when the owner is foreign; why the rules moved and what is coming.

Reference study, not legal or tax advice. Every statement carries its status as of 6 October 2026 and a number that leads to the memory record behind it and to the document that record was read in. Prices are firms' own published prices on the dates given, not a tariff; times are published times, not commitments; nothing is added up into a total. The last section lists what was not established and when to look again.

gid research round cyprus-setup-guide · 6 October 2026 · 472 records stored in three waves, 79.7% read in the primary document · sixteen research slices, each checked by two adversarial verifiers: 277 of 385 researcher facts corrected, 4 refuted · written from the records by seven writers and checked item by item by seven checkers, who corrected 198 of 548 items · revised 7 October 2026 for the reader's eleven comments: a fourth wave of 106 records (102 new, 4 corrected editions; 82.1% read in the primary document), then six writers and six checkers, who corrected 105 of 231 new or changed items

  1. Question 1Four rungs: each adds filings, and only the company limits every member's liabilityAs of 6 October 2026 a person may trade with no Registrar filing; a business name adds €10 and €80 and no yearly return; a partnership €120; a company an advocate, a €165 filing, a yearly return and audit or review. In return the company limits members' liability, within statutory exceptions, and a working shareholder is insured as an employed person.
  2. Path 1Path 1: Social Insurance and a tax number; the Registrar only for a trade nameA self-employed person registers with the Social Insurance Services by the day work starts and enters the Tax Register; a trade name adds name approval and form EE1 (€10 and €80). No step found is reserved to an advocate. An EU citizen may start before his registration certificate; a third-country national first needs a status that allows it.
  3. Path 2A private company: advocate-drafted papers and HE1 at the Registrar, then tax, owners, VAT, staffFormed at the Registrar after name approval, on a memorandum and articles only an advocate may draft; the Registrar's HE1 is an advocate's oath, though the Law also allows a director or secretary. Tax registration via CY Login (within 60 days), beneficial owners (90 days) and VAT when due follow. As of 6 October 2026 no authority publishes processing times.
  4. Path 3Selling online to consumers: no licence aimed at it, but site duties, VAT and producer dutiesNo licence is aimed at selling online. A web shop adds information and withdrawal duties to consumers, product-safety details on each offer, VAT for sales into other EU states (the One Stop Shop only after Cyprus VAT registration) and, where the shop itself imports or first supplies the goods, producer, customs or cosmetics steps.
  5. Path 4A shop needs a municipal licence; a café adds tourism and health approvals and missing use permitsIn a municipality a shop needs the s. 56 premises licence; four months' silence after the town has all the papers it asks for (they differ by town) counts as a grant. It pays a yearly trade fee the Law caps. A café adds missing catering-use permits, a sanitary certificate, an approved manager and the tourism licence; alcohol and sound are licensed apart.
  6. Question 2Real cases 2022-2026: register and web dates are public; no licence issue date was foundRead from the Registrar's free search, web archives, the press and the tourism lists as of 6 October 2026. Two of the barest businesses were public before their registration; the tourism lists seen print at most a licence's expiry, no issue date; B-READY 2025's 11.5 days for company entry is an expert median, not a case.
  7. Question 3Only an advocate may draft a company's founding documents; later routine filings name no lawyerCyprus law reserves drafting a company's memorandum and articles to a practising advocate, audits to licensed auditors, plans to registered engineers and architects, and director, secretary and office services offered to others to eligible persons. Firms publish formation prices on different bases as of 6 October 2026; none is an official fee.
  8. The foreign elementA foreign founder files the same company forms; permits, papers, screening and banks add the restThe Registrar's criteria identify founders by passport or ID number and ask for no residence permit; per an agency report, she seeks other services' views on companies with Turkish or Turkish Cypriot officers. The foreign element shows mainly in the right to run a business, investor permits, papers from abroad, branches, screening, land, sanctions and banks.
  9. WhyWhy rules changed: reasons sit in bills, committee reports and speeches; some changes state noneStated reasons come from Government reports to the House, MPs' memoranda, committee reports, a Minister's speech and the Fiscal Council; press accounts fill gaps. The 15% corporate rate has two accounts. The 2026 strike-off drive, the 2018 shell-company circular and the minimum-wage decree state no reason.
  10. What is comingWhat is coming: Cyprus bills mostly undated, EU dates fixed, several transpositions lateAs of 6 October 2026 the Cyprus changes that touch a founder are bills, drafts or plans, and few have a dated next step. EU acts set fixed dates; for consumer credit, the single permit, pay transparency and beneficial-owner register access the Cyprus deadline had passed with no law found. Nothing here is in force unless a row says so.
  11. CalendarCalendar to 2028: fixed deadlines of adopted acts and meetings where change may be announcedEach entry says whether it is a date set by an adopted act, a meeting at which something may be announced, a forecast or an expectation. Dates marked 'counted' are the researchers' arithmetic from the act. Entries before 6 October 2026 are the recent dates the guide relies on.
  12. Not establishedOpen questions: what the round did not establish, where it looked, and when to check againForty-four questions a founder would act on, each with what the records do establish as of 6 October 2026, where the answer was looked for, what would close it and a recheck date. They are grouped by the guide section they belong to.
Question 1 · The ladder: what each kind of registration takes and gives

Four rungs: each adds filings, and only the company limits every member's liability

As of 6 October 2026 a person may trade with no Registrar filing; a business name adds €10 and €80 and no yearly return; a partnership €120; a company an advocate, a €165 filing, a yearly return and audit or review. In return the company limits members' liability, within statutory exceptions, and a working shareholder is insured as an employed person.

A natural person may trade in Cyprus without forming a company. The Registrar of Companies' comparison table sets 'unlimited liability of the owner of a business name' against 'limited liability of company members' (read 4 October 2026). The steps are in path 1 (self-employed) and path 2 (company).1

The rungs at a glance (as of 6 October 2026)

RungWho may use itWhat it takesFiled or paid every yearGives over the rung below
Self-employed, own nameA natural person; the Social Insurance rule sets no nationality or residence condition. A third-country national first needs a status that allows self-employment (path 1).Social Insurance by the day work starts (number 'usually' in 1-2 weeks, per the business portal); Tax Register through Tax For All ('may take up to ten (10) days', Tax Department guide). Nothing at the Registrar.Social insurance in 2026: 16.6% each quarter on income from a category minimum up to €1,325 a week; GeSY (the General Healthcare System) 4%; training levy 0.5% from 6 April 2026; tax return; books, but from tax year 2026 no audited or reviewed accounts while turnover and other gross income stay within €120,000.The barest rung, yet it can take cards; for consumers he must, on the law's definitions, if his main activity code is listed. Per providers' texts (4-6 Oct 2026) JCC, myPOS, SumUp, Viva (general guide) and Revolut Pro take a natural person, Shopify Payments and Stripe one living in Cyprus; Wise took no new card clients.123456789101112131415161718192021
Business nameA natural or legal person with a Cyprus place of business, trading under a name other than his surname with forenames or initials; Cap. 116 sets no nationality or residence condition. The Registrar's criteria ask a non-EU owner for the Immigration Officer's permission - a title from the immigration Regulations (below).Name approval €10, then within one month of starting business form EE1 (the business-name statement) or e-filing, €80; €20 and €40 more if accelerated. No advocate's declaration is asked; no examination time is published (Registrar and Ministry texts read 4-6 October 2026).No annual return or yearly fee at the Registrar; notices of change within seven days.A registered trade name; Bank of Cyprus's business onboarding form covers a business name and Wise's verification article has a business-name route (read 4-6 October 2026). Liability stays unlimited.1222324252627282930
Partnership, general or limitedPartners trading together, 'up to 100' per the Registrar; a legal person may be a limited partner.Name approval, then within one month of formation e-filing or form Σ1 signed by all partners, €120 (€40 more accelerated); beneficial-owner details filed.Books; a partnership with business income has its accounts audited or reviewed for tax unless the Commissioner exempts it; each partner's share in his own return, plus a return of the firm's income; an annual return only where the sole or all general partners are limited companies or partnerships.Several owners in one firm; a limited partner liable only up to his contribution, but at least one general partner liable for all debts.3132333435
Private company limited by sharesOne or more persons; at least one director; Cap. 113 sets no residence or nationality requirement for a director or secretary.Name approval €10; memorandum and articles by a practising advocate; HE1 (the incorporation declaration), in practice sworn by the advocate; filing €165 with share capital. Online, the Law sets 5 or 10 working days, else reasons (s. 366Ε(5)); no actual times published. Tax Register within 60 days, beneficial owners within 90.Annual return (€20) within 28 days of its date; accounts audited, or reviewed within €300,000 turnover and €500,000 assets two years running; beneficial-owner confirmation 1 October-31 December; tax return even without income; municipal legal-person fee.Members' liability limited, with exceptions; a shareholder working for it insured as an employed person; group relief of losses; the Research and Innovation Foundation's PRE-SEED grant expected a registered company (2023 FAQ).10263637383940414243444546474849505152

Fees as the Registrar published them; duties as the Laws read on 6 October 2026.

What the Immigration Officer's permission is. The Registrar's criteria for business-name form EE1 ask it of a non-EU owner, naming no law or document for it, and the paper form has no field labelled residence permit or immigration permission. 'Immigration Officer' is how the Aliens and Immigration Regulations define their officer, the 'Τμηματάρχης', who decides a third-country national's one-year Regulation 12 permit for an 'independent profession': running his own business, alone or as a company or partnership, with no employer. Long-term residents and EU citizens' family members have self-employment by law instead. Read together (5-6 October 2026), no text says what the Registrar accepts.323305354555657

In one business-name case, VIES showed the VAT number held by the owner, not the name (6 October 2026). What VAT registration means for a founder (as of 6 October 2026)

  • Registration attaches to the person - the individual, the partnership or the company - not to the form: the VAT Law's 'taxable person' is everyone registered or required to be. For one sole trader with a business name, the EU's VIES check on 6 October 2026 showed the VAT number held by the owner himself, not by the name.6567
  • Compulsory for a person established in Cyprus once taxable supplies in the 12 months to a month's end exceed €15,600: notice within 30 days of that month's end; registered from the end of the next month or an agreed earlier date, unless the Commissioner accepts the coming year stays below a lower limit. Expecting to pass €15,600 within 30 days: notice before they end. Capital assets aside; late notice €85 a month.6692
  • Other triggers: services to businesses in other EU states that account for the VAT there - registration from the first one, with no threshold, plus VIES statements; and goods bought from other EU states above €10,251.61 in a calendar year.66
  • Voluntary: below the threshold he may register; one who carries on business and intends to make taxable supplies may register before his first sale, on written request with his identity card and evidence of activity or of the right to register. Form T.F. 1101 goes on paper to a district office (Tax Department's registration page, 6 October 2026) or, per the business portal, to the Point of Single Contact in Nicosia.6693949596
  • Rates on taxable sales: 19% standard; 9%, 5% or 3% on listed supplies; zero on exports and, for a time, some basic goods (4 October 2026). A registered seller hands a consumer in Cyprus a 'legal receipt' at the time of sale, showing his VAT number, the total with VAT and the rate; a retailer owes a VAT invoice only if a customer who is a taxable person asks.979899
  • Returns go through Tax For All, normally for quarters set at registration by activity code; each return and its payment fall due by the 10th day of the second month after the period (Tax Department's 2026 calendar). VAT records are kept six years unless the Commissioner says otherwise.100101
  • Non-exempt services bought from abroad for the business: per a 2010 VAT circular still listed by the Tax Department on 4 October 2026, the business accounts for their VAT itself (reverse charge), and one not registered 'must register'; the Law has no separate trigger. Per the return guide, that VAT goes in as output tax and may go in as input tax under the Law's conditions: a fully taxable business comes out even.102103104105
  • Selling to consumers in other EU states, for a seller established only in Cyprus: goods sent there and telecom, broadcasting or electronic services stay taxed in Cyprus while they total no more than €10,000 net of VAT in the current and the preceding calendar year; above that, the VAT of the customer's state is due.106
  • The One Stop Shop, which traders may use but need not, takes registration and quarterly returns for those sales on the Cyprus Tax Department's OSS portal (the Department's OSS page), under the VAT number Cyprus already gave the seller - so, on the VAT Directive's wording, after Cyprus VAT registration.106107108109

How a self-employed person takes cards (texts read 4-6 October 2026; no sign-up tested)

  • Whether he must: Law 4/1978 makes the 'payee' 'the natural or legal person' finally receiving the funds, and decree K.D.P. 259/2021, as amended to 2022, binds payees whose main activity code it lists to accept consumers' cards; business clients' payments are outside. No text names the self-employed: that the duty reaches him is a reading of the definitions. A listed business starting now has one month to comply.1269110
  • The list takes in most in-store retail, restaurants and bars, legal and accounting work, architecture, engineering consultancy, specialised design (74.10), photography, veterinary, medical and dental practice and computer repair; it leaves out retail by mail order or internet (47.91), computer programming, IT consultancy and management consultancy. No amendment after 2022 shows in cylaw's indexes (6 October 2026).69110111
  • What it takes: a contract with a licensed payment service provider or its agent, and four-party scheme cards taken by POS, mobile or virtual POS; bank transfer, direct debit or e-banking does not count. Notices at the entrance and the till. No business bank account, business name or company is required; the Tax Department's directive 20/2021 applies the duty regardless of amount, turnover and VAT registration.70112113
  • JCC (Become a merchant): its form (v2.3, 4 October 2026) is signed, stamped and e-mailed with ID or passport, proof of home and business address, an IBAN certificate and, for a third-country national, a CY/EU permit; JCC may ask for more; €17 once. June 2026 copies show a 'Natural Person' category, a mandatory stamp and takings paid the next business day to a current account at any local bank; terminals rented.1371114115
  • myPOS's Cyprus agreement (3 April 2026) admits a sole trader on his personal address, credits takings to his myPOS e-money account and bars personal use unrelated to the business. SumUp's Cyprus help centre has a sole-trader route: ID and selfie at sign-up, and it may ask proof of home address and of business activity. Viva's general, not Cyprus, guide has 'freelancer accounts', naming no Cyprus number for one.141621116
  • Revolut's payment terms (versions applying from 17 November 2025 and from 28 September 2026) cover a 'freelancer' or 'sole trader' applying through a Revolut Pro account: if accepted, card acceptance is switched on in it and takings are paid into it, and Revolut 'will always withhold' a reserve of 0-100% of each transaction for a period at its discretion. Its Cyprus help page on Pro was seen only in a 2025 copy.17117118
  • Shopify Payments (Cyprus requirements) takes an 'Individual / Sole Trader' living in Cyprus, registered with a government agency or not, and lists no tax or VAT number for that type; payouts go to a euro SEPA account, not a savings, multi-currency or money-transfer one. An account opened from June 2026 must finish verification within 90 days of its first order, or orders may be refunded.18119
  • Stripe lets only someone living in Cyprus hold an individual or sole-proprietor account there (read 4 October 2026) and lists its in-person Terminal for Cyprus only as a preview. Wise's card-acceptance page said on 6 October 2026 that the feature 'is currently unavailable to new Wise Business customers'.1920120
  • A personal account? The card rules set none. JCC credits a current account at any local bank and asks the merchant name 'exactly as it appears on the Bank system' (June 2026 copies); SumUp takes a euro-area personal account in the profile holder's name, though it calls separating personal and business money 'necessary'; Stripe checks the account name. Eurobank's personal Current Account lists the self-employed.13157071121122

Where a company's limited liability stops

  • Fraudulent trading: where in a winding up business is found carried on to defraud creditors, the Court, on application of the Official Receiver, the liquidator or a creditor or contributory, may declare persons knowingly party to it personally responsible, 'without any limitation of liability', for all or any of the company's debts as it directs (Cap. 113 s. 311).123
  • Tax, since 1 January 2026: officers who bear criminal liability for a company's unpaid tax (for tax it withheld, its executive director, board members and financial officers; for other taxes only if shown to have taken part fraudulently) are also liable jointly or severally with it in civil proceedings (Law 4/1978 s. 51A(3A)); a director deleted from the register stays answerable for liabilities of his term (s. 53A).124125
  • Social insurance and consumer law: an officer whose consent, connivance or neglect lies behind the company's offence is guilty of it too (Law 59(I)/2010 s. 85(9); Law 112(I)/2021 s. 65).126127
  • Beneficial owners: the director or managing director is jointly and/or severally liable for the charge on a company that fails to file (anti-money-laundering (AML) Law s. 61A(10)(ε)).128

The yearly cost of standing still

  • Self-employed, assembled from the Social Insurance Services' 2026 Tables I and II: on the lowest notional minimum (€331.14 a week, category 13, which includes 'persons responsible for providing services and sellers') social insurance comes to €2,858.40 a year, GeSY €688.77 and the training levy from the second quarter €64.57 - about €3,612.129130
  • Shopkeepers' minimum is €452.56 a week (€3,906.48 social insurance a year); that of liberal professionals in their first ten years, designers, computer operators and anyone not classed elsewhere €485.67 (€4,192.32). The base runs from the category minimum unless lower income is proved; form YKA 1-017 is for paying on actual income.85129130131
  • Business name: no yearly Registrar fee. Municipal premises, any rung: a yearly trade fee. The Municipalities Law sets only ceilings (€50 self-employed craftsmen; €200 professionals, traders and businessmen working as individuals; €150-500 a private company or partnership, by staff); the council sets category and amount. No published amounts found for Nicosia, Larnaca, Paphos or Limassol (6 October 2026); see path 4.27132133134135136137
  • Company: annual return €20; late, €50 plus €1 a day, at most €150 (the Registrar's fee page still showed the older €500 scheme on 4 October 2026); a municipal legal-person fee with ceilings of €1,000 up to 5 employees and €4,000 where its registered office or management is with a law, accounting or audit firm or an administrative-service provider (Limassol, from 20 June 2023: €222, €127 in a group).4549138139
  • Company accounts, per firms: review €600-1,500, audit €1,200-3,500+ (Savva Cyprus, 14 July 2026); a dormant company all-in roughly €1,250-2,150 a year (Zeno, 3 July 2026).140
  • Of 12,583 companies registered in 2022, the Registrar's open data of 29 September 2026 show 7,459 (59.3%) 'registered', 3,532 (28.1%) 'reminder letter sent' - 3,518 of these dated February 2026, the month of its letters on overdue returns and accounts - and 1,010 (8.0%) struck off.141
Source trap

Several Registrar guidance pages still described the €350 annual company levy as current on 4 October 2026. Law 25(I)/2024 abolished it from 1 January 2024 but kept the obligations for 2011-2023: per its notice of 30 September 2026, in December 2026 the Registrar will begin striking off companies that did not pay it for any of 2012-2023 - publication in the Gazette for three months, then removal unless cause to the contrary is shown.142143144

Source trap

The Social Insurance Services' 2026 Table II, as served on 6 October 2026, dates the self-employed training levy 'from 5.1.2026'; Law 15(I)/2026 and Regulations K.D.P. 128/2026 (K.D.P.: the Cyprus numbering of regulatory acts) started it on 6 April 2026. From 5 January it would be €86.10 on the lowest minimum, not €64.57.9129145

New registrations by legal form (chart) come from the Registrar's year-end charts, which give no split by activity and count a business name whether its owner is a person or a company; counted from the Registrar's open data, 12,452 of the 12,583 companies of 2022 were private. Eurostat's enterprise births in industry, construction and market services, units that become active, are not comparable: among them sole proprietorships outnumber limited liability enterprises in each year 2022-2024.146147148149

Path 1 · Trading in one's own name

Path 1: Social Insurance and a tax number; the Registrar only for a trade name

A self-employed person registers with the Social Insurance Services by the day work starts and enters the Tax Register; a trade name adds name approval and form EE1 (€10 and €80). No step found is reserved to an advocate. An EU citizen may start before his registration certificate; a third-country national first needs a status that allows it.

  1. 1Social Insurance registrationRegulations: by the day work starts; business portal: number 'usually' within 1-2 weeks
  2. 2Name approval, for a trade name onlynone published (Registrar and Ministry texts read 4-6 October 2026)
  3. 3Business-name registrationCap. 116: within one month of starting business
  4. 4Tax Register entryTax Department guide: 'may take up to ten (10) days', calendar or working days not said
  5. 5VAT, when due or by choiceVAT Law: notify within 30 days from the end of the month the threshold was passed, or before the end of the 30 days in which it is expected to be
  6. 6Card acceptance: possible in his own name, a duty for listed activitiesdecree: within one month of starting
  7. 7CY Login account, for the services that sign in through itno document places it in path 1

Path 1 in order of doing (as of 6 October 2026)

  1. Social Insurance registrationRegulations: by the day work starts; business portal: number 'usually' within 1-2 weeks
    Form YKA 1-013 or the joint YKA 1-008 (forms page); no text read says if one or both. Paper goes to the nearest District Social Insurance Office (table below); the business portal also allows posting YKA 1-008 with a copy of an ID card, passport or alien registration certificate (ARC). The Services' gov.cy page offers one e-route: YKA 1-013 eID-signed and e-mailed; by law the eID goes only to Cypriot citizens. The rule sets no nationality or residence condition; it counts as training-levy registration too.24145151152153154
  2. Name approval, for a trade name onlynone published (Registrar and Ministry texts read 4-6 October 2026)
    Needed where he has a place of business in Cyprus and trades under a name other than his surname with his forenames or initials. Online: the Registrar's name approval e-form, which opens the CY Login sign-in; results come through e-services. Or by hand or post to the Registrar's cashiers in Nicosia (table below). €10 per name, €20 more accelerated; the name stays reserved six months. No document sets an order between this step and Social Insurance registration.1222526155156157
  3. Business-name registrationCap. 116: within one month of starting business
    After approval. Online: the Registrar's e-filing with a CY Login account, €80 by credit card, and a free witness's solemn declaration that he signed EE1 before the witness; the texts ask no officer to certify it. Or form EE1 by hand (cash, cheque or transfer) or post to the Nicosia cashiers. €40 more accelerated; plus any consent required. The statement gives the name, nature, principal place, start, and his names, nationality, usual residence. The €9 advocates' stamp is charged 'for the filing by an advocate'.1222454155156157158159160
  4. Tax Register entryTax Department guide: 'may take up to ten (10) days', calendar or working days not said
    Online only. He opens an account at Tax For All sign-up with personal and contact details, activated by e-mail; it asks no Cypriot identity or alien registration number, and a CY Login account may be used instead. Then 'register as a new taxpayer', uploading the documents; the number comes by e-mail. A declared trade name needs the business-name certificate attached, so then this step follows the one above. No document orders it against Social Insurance. The Law sets an individual no day-count deadline.56161162163
  5. VAT, when due or by choiceVAT Law: notify within 30 days from the end of the month the threshold was passed, or before the end of the 30 days in which it is expected to be
    Due for a person established in Cyprus once taxable supplies in the 12 months to a month's end exceed €15,600 (unless the Commissioner accepts the next year stays under the lower limit), or are expected to within 30 days; voluntary on intention. Paper only: form T.F. 1101 with an ID card copy and proof of activity, at a district VAT office or, by hand or post, the Point of Single Contact (table below). Form and web pages differ on whether a pending tax-number reference (REGXXX) will do. Late notice €85 a month.669293949596164
  6. Card acceptance: possible in his own name, a duty for listed activitiesdecree: within one month of starting
    No text names him; the law's payee is 'the natural or legal person', so on the definitions the decree binds him for consumer payments if his main activity code is listed (most shops, restaurants, law, accounting, medicine; not online retail or programming). The rules need no company, trade name or business account. JCC ('Natural Person' category in a June 2026 form copy; tax, VAT fields; current account; €17), myPOS, SumUp, Viva (general guide), Revolut Pro, Shopify Payments take him (read 4-6 Oct 2026; untested).12131415161718216970111114165
  7. CY Login account, for the services that sign in through itno document places it in path 1
    The Registrar's online name approval and e-filing sign in through it, SISnet is being linked to it, Tax For All works without it. Sign-up: CY Login registration; the profile is then identified by a bank's e-banking, by video call (Cypriot ID holders only), or in person by appointment at a Citizen Service Centre (KEP) or Cyprus Post centre (KE.PO.) (table below), with form CYL5-NP and an original Cypriot ID card, residence permit with the ARC, or EU registration certificate; a passport alone is not on the list.5155157161166167168169170

Source trap look again by 6 Jan 2027

If he also employs staff (form YKA 1-001 has a block for a natural-person employer): the Social Insurance Services' Employer Guide (PDF of 2 June 2025, on a page updated 30 September 2026) says that form may be handed in at any District Social Insurance Office or at a Citizen Service Centre (KEP). The KEP list of services of 1 December 2025, read 6 October 2026, has 30 Social Insurance items, and none is the registration of an employer, an employee or a self-employed person. Neither text addresses the other.586162

A business name then carries standing duties under Cap. 116: changes notified within seven days, cessation within one month; letters show the owner's name and, if he is not Cypriot, his nationality. A late notice of a new place of business or changed particulars costs up to €50 plus €1 a day, at most €250; while in default the owner cannot enforce the business's contracts by action without a court's relief.27

Contributions can be paid electronically in SISnet, the Services' payment system, which they now link to a CY Login profile: step-by-step guides, one for 'a self-employed person who is not an employer', are on their site under 26 August 2026 and were announced on gov.cy on 7 September 2026. On 6 October 2026 the SISnet page still showed user-name and password fields and gave no date for the change. Per the Services' notice of 25 September 2026, users having difficulty registering on CY Login may pay at the cashiers of the District Social Insurance Offices until Friday 30 October 2026, without extension of the normal payment deadline; district officers will help with CY Login identification.166167

An EU citizen's branch

  • He may start before the registration certificate: Law 7(I)/2007 art. 23(1) bars making it a precondition where entitlement can be shown by other means.180
  • Staying over three months, he files registration form MEU1 in person within four months of arrival (fine up to €2,500; fee €20). Nicosia district: Migration Department; its Appointments page does not list MEU1 as needing one, others go first come first served. Elsewhere: police unit, by e-mail appointment (table above). Decision 'within one month at the latest' on complete papers (Department); the Law: 'immediately'.177178179181
  • For the self-employed ground the Department's list (5 October 2026) asks for a certificate of contributions paid for the last four months, or, if he is insured in another EU state, form A1 and health insurance; the Law asks, with an identity card or passport and the €20 fee, only 'proof of self-employment'; the business portal names a certificate of registration as self-employed.182183184185
  • Tax Register: the certificate; without it or a residence permit, category 4 papers - a foreign tax-number certificate, identity card or social-insurance certificate, a photo and a letter of reasons with evidence - plus the fee receipt if he applied. The page (28 September 2026) lists evidence for property, a foreign director, the 60-day rule and employment, none for self-employment, nor says it is accepted.186187
  • Read together, the texts mean that without a commercial bank's e-banking he can have a CY Login profile identified in person only once he holds the MEU1 certificate, as a passport alone is not on CY Login's list. CY Login also offers EU citizens 'Sign in with eIDAS'; neither its page nor the Deputy Ministry's announcement of 17 March 2025 says which services accept it.169170188
Open look again by 6 Apr 2027

The Department asks a self-employed EU citizen for four months of paid contributions (or form A1 and health insurance if insured in another EU state), while the Law has him apply within four months of arrival; no text read on 5-6 October 2026 reconciles the two periods.181182183

A third-country national needs a status allowing his own business. The Aliens and Immigration Regulations treat it as an 'independent profession', alone or via a company or partnership, needing a permit; a visitor's permit bars any business without the Immigration Officer's prior written permission; an employment permit, work only for the named employer. For a non-EU owner the Registrar's EE1 criteria say 'permission is required by the Immigration Officer' (Greek 'Λειτουργός Μετανάστευσης'), naming no law or document. The Regulations' 'Τμηματάρχης', defined as 'the Immigration Officer in the Immigration Service…', decides the independent-profession permit and a visitor's permission.323305355189190191

At the other counters a third-country national shows his own documents: form YKA 1-013 takes an alien registration or passport number; the Tax Register takes the residence permit, all sides, or, if only applied for, the fee receipt plus the foreign certificate, photograph and letter of reasons.131186

Two or more people trading together register a partnership within one month of formation: name approval, then e-filing or form Σ1 signed by all partners, €120 (€40 more accelerated). Every partner is jointly liable for the firm's debts; limited partners only up to their contribution. A limited partnership is not a separate legal person (Cap. 116 s. 47(6)); the Ministry's 2026 action plan schedules drafting a framework for limited partnerships 'with separate legal personality' (September 2025-January 2027); no such bill was found on the consultation portal, in the Gazette or in CyLaw by 6 October 2026.313235204

Path 2 · The barest company

A private company: advocate-drafted papers and HE1 at the Registrar, then tax, owners, VAT, staff

Formed at the Registrar after name approval, on a memorandum and articles only an advocate may draft; the Registrar's HE1 is an advocate's oath, though the Law also allows a director or secretary. Tax registration via CY Login (within 60 days), beneficial owners (90 days) and VAT when due follow. As of 6 October 2026 no authority publishes processing times.

  1. 1Name approvalRegistrar: none published; per firms 3-5 working days or about two weeks standard, 1-4 working days accelerated
  2. 2Memorandum and articles
  3. 3Signatures
  4. 4HE1 declaration
  5. 5Filingstatute, online route only: 5 or 10 working days (Cap. 113 s. 366Ε(5)); Registrar: none published
  6. 6Certificates

Forming the company, in the order the Registrar's texts set

  1. Name approvalRegistrar: none published; per firms 3-5 working days or about two weeks standard, 1-4 working days accelerated
    An e-filing user first registers on the gov.cy gateway, whose links lead to CY Login (Registrar page, read 5 October 2026). €10 per proposed name, €20 more accelerated; an approved name stays reserved for six months, within which the incorporation is filed (as of 4 October 2026).2538157
  2. Memorandum and articles
    Only a practising advocate may draft them, certifying that he did; a memorandum or articles drafted in breach is void (Advocates Law, Cap. 2, s. 11(5)). The Registrar offers a model memorandum for a general commercial company (2018). His review criteria require the name 'as approved', so the memorandum follows name approval.39217218
  3. Signatures
    The shareholders sign the memorandum and articles in Greek. The memorandum is dated on or before the day HE1 is submitted; the articles bear the same date and the drafting lawyer's name and signature. Online, a witness declares on the Registrar's Annex 'A' that the shareholders and the officer signing forms HE2 and HE3 signed before him, as did the drafting advocate.218219220
  4. HE1 declaration
    HE1, the incorporation declaration form, asks for the name-approval application number; no text read orders it against the signatures. The Registrar's form is an advocate's oath before a District Court registrar (table below); a missing lawyer's stamp is listed first among the 'most common reasons for rejection'.218221222
  5. Filingstatute, online route only: 5 or 10 working days (Cap. 113 s. 366Ε(5)); Registrar: none published
    Any registered e-filing user may submit HE1, the memorandum and articles and the witness's declaration; on paper the Registrar lists HE1, HE2 (office), HE3 (officers) and the memorandum and articles. Fee €165 (€235 without share capital), €100 more accelerated (as of 6 October 2026). The statutory days (5 where only natural persons form it on the Registrar's templates, 10 otherwise) run from completed formalities and payment; if they cannot be met, the Registrar gives the reasons.2642219220
  6. Certificates
    Optional set of five (shareholders, officers, office, memorandum and articles, incorporation): €120, or €130 without a supplied copy, €100 more accelerated (as of 4 October 2026). The tax-register request attaches four.223224225

Open look again by 6 Apr 2027

Since Law 151(I)/2023 (Gazette 19 December 2023) the Companies Law provides for forming a company 'entirely by electronic means': s. 366Ε has the Registrar issue binding directives that must cover 'the role of the practising advocate ... subject to the Advocates Law', and s. 366ΣΤ has him post template memoranda and articles. On 6 October 2026 the Registrar's page still asks on the e-filing route for HE1 sworn by the entrusted lawyer; cylaw's indexes for 2023-2026 list no directive on online formation.230

Directors, secretary, registered office

  • A private company has at least one director and a secretary; a sole director may be secretary only in a single-member company (Cap. 113 s. 171(1)). Sections 170-172 set no residence, nationality or licensing condition; the Registrar's guidance adds a minimum age of 18. A legal person may hold either post, save two combinations s. 172 forbids. The office is in Cyprus (s. 102), in Greek, not 'a mailbox' (Registrar).37231232233
  • Providing directors, a secretary or an office address as a service is reserved to CySEC-licensed companies, advocates, ICPAC members and their firms (Law 196(I)/2012). Unlicensed, one may direct a company at least 25% owned with family or, if resident in Cyprus, be secretary of one at least 50% so owned (25% if all shareholders are Cyprus-resident individuals) or of one's sole employer, if not advertised or offered.234235236
  • Law 196(I)/2012 gives no like exemption for providing a registered office address; whether a founder giving his own address to his own company 'provides' that service, the Act does not say (cylaw, read 6 October 2026).236

The first years

  • Annual return HE32I, e-filed with the financial statements within 28 days of its reference date, the first being the day after 18 months from incorporation: for a company registered on 1 October 2026 the Registrar's calculator gives reference date 2 April 2028 and deadline 30 April 2028; fee €20 (as of 4 October 2026).45250
  • Beneficial-owner details are confirmed every 1 October-31 December; per the Registrar's notice of 16 September 2026, a company whose 90-day deadline or first filing falls in the 2026 window also confirms by 31 December 2026.44251
Not there look again by 6 Jan 2027

As of 6 October 2026 no Cyprus authority publishes how long name approval or incorporation takes. Read: the Registrar's guidance, statistics and publications pages (its monthly charts count applications, not durations), the gov.cy name-approval service page, the business portal, the Ministry's annual report for 2023 and its action plans. The World Bank's experts likewise answered that incorporation service standards are not online. Firms' figures differ and cite no source.2526252

The World Bank's B-READY 2025 gives an aggregate of expert answers, not a statute or a register count: 11.5 calendar days and 1,920 euro (1,750 foreign-owned), VAT excluded, for the most common limited company in the largest city, with employees and turnover above the VAT threshold (reference point 1 September 2024). Each figure is a separate median, and some steps are marked as done at the same time: the per-step medians below sum to 20.5 days and 2,055. No bank account is counted.253254255256257

What is known on each point, and where it was looked for, is in the register at the end.

Path 3 · Selling to consumers online

Selling online to consumers: no licence aimed at it, but site duties, VAT and producer duties

No licence is aimed at selling online. A web shop adds information and withdrawal duties to consumers, product-safety details on each offer, VAT for sales into other EU states (the One Stop Shop only after Cyprus VAT registration) and, where the shop itself imports or first supplies the goods, producer, customs or cosmetics steps.

Pursuing an information-society service needs no prior authorisation, without prejudice to authorisation schemes not aimed specifically at such services (Electronic Commerce Law 156(I)/2004, s. 7(1); in force as read on 5 October 2026). Per the business portal (read 6 October 2026), alcohol and tobacco need a sale licence from the municipality where the premises are, or else the District Officer; food supplements a Ministry of Health notification or, for some products, licence.260261

What the shop's pages and contracts carry

  • The provider's name, geographic address, contact details with e-mail, register and registration number, the supervisory authority where approval is needed, and the VAT number where VAT applies; prices say whether tax and delivery are included (Electronic Commerce Law s. 8).260
  • Per the Consumer Protection Service's page (undated, footer 2022, read 5 October 2026): pre-contract information, confirmation on a durable medium, 14 days to withdraw (from delivery for goods, from the contract for services; up to a year more if the information was not given), refund within 14 days of notice, delivery within 30 days unless agreed. It names no registration and omits the Law's 2026 amendments.262
  • A trader selling goods to consumers shows the selling price and the unit price including VAT and all taxes, in euro; an advertised price comes with its unit price; a reduction states the lowest price of the previous 30 days, save for goods that perish or expire quickly and goods on the market under 30 days (Consumer Protection Law 112(I)/2021, ss. 42-45).263
  • The Consumer Protection Service's Director may fine a breach of that Law up to 5% of the previous year's turnover, or €3,000,000 where turnover is unknown, plus up to €5,000 a day while it continues; recourse does not suspend payment (ss. 57, 61). An officer whose consent, connivance or neglect is shown is also guilty (s. 65).127264

What changed for online sellers, 2024-2026

SinceWasNowActWeight

  1. 1Cyprus VATVAT Law: notify within 30 days after the month the threshold was passed
  2. 2Sales to consumers elsewhere in the EU
  3. 3OSSRegulation 282/2011 art. 57d: next quarter after notice, or first supply if notified by the 10th of the next month

VAT: Cyprus registration first, the One Stop Shop only if chosen

  1. Cyprus VATVAT Law: notify within 30 days after the month the threshold was passed
    For a person established in Cyprus, due once taxable supplies in the past 12 months exceed €15,600 (unless the Commissioner accepts the coming year will stay below the lower limit), or before the end of a 30-day period in which they are expected to exceed it; allowed earlier, on intention. Intra-EU acquisitions of goods above €10,251.61 a calendar year are a separate trigger (Tax Department page, read 4 October 2026).669293
  2. Sales to consumers elsewhere in the EU
    A seller established only in Cyprus taxes its intra-EU distance sales of goods, and of telecom, broadcasting and electronic services, to consumers in Cyprus while they stay within €10,000 (net of VAT) in the current and the preceding calendar year; above that, at the customer's state's VAT (Directive 2006/112/EC art. 59c, applied from 1 July 2021), as also when the seller opts for it. The One Stop Shop (OSS) is optional: states 'shall permit' traders to use it (art. 369b).106107
  3. OSSRegulation 282/2011 art. 57d: next quarter after notice, or first supply if notified by the 10th of the next month
    OSS uses 'the individual VAT identification number already allocated' (art. 369d), so Cyprus VAT comes first. Sign-up and quarterly returns, due by the end of the month after the quarter, are on the Tax Department's portal tax-oss.mof.gov.cy, per the Commission's Cyprus page, which does not mention Tax For All.106107108109269

The duty to accept cards from consumers turns on the main activity code (Decree K.D.P. 259/2021 under art. 30A of Law 4/1978): most store retail codes 47.11-47.78 are listed, retail via mail order or internet (47.91) is not, and no amending decree adds it (cylaw's indexes to K.D.P. 372/2026, read 6 October 2026). A listed business starting later complies within one month of starting; a virtual POS counts, credit transfers do not.69112270

Breaching the Packaging Law's duties or its Regulations is an offence: up to three years' imprisonment or €85,500, or both; out of court, up to €3,400, doubled for a repeat or a breach not ended within 48 hours (Law 32(I)/2002, ss. 26, 26A).288

Source trap

The Department of Environment's EPR page (updated 29 September 2026) speaks of packaging producers 'in volumes at least, up to 2 tonnes per material per year'. The phrase is garbled old text; no such threshold is in Law 32(I)/2002 or K.D.P. 747/2003.289

A Cyprus producer selling EEE at a distance into another Member State appoints an authorised representative there (K.D.P. 73/2015, reg. 22(2)); EU law sets a like duty for packaging (Regulation (EU) 2025/40, art. 45(3)) and batteries (Regulation (EU) 2023/1542, art. 56(3)). Commission proposals of 10 December 2025 to suspend these duties until 1 January 2035 were not adopted as of 20 September 2026; per the Parliament's Legislative Train, the committee vote was planned for early October 2026.274290291

Other steps by product

  • Imports from outside the EU: customs registration (EORI, Union Customs Code art. 9(1)) on form C.1000 at a District Customs office, with the Registrar certificates and the VAT certificate 'if any', so after incorporation, VAT not required. The form (2019) states no fee and no processing time.292293
  • Cosmetics imported from outside the EU: the shop is the 'responsible person' for each product unless it mandates another EU-established person in writing; the responsible person notifies the Commission electronically before placing it on the market (Regulation (EC) No 1223/2009, arts 4(5), 13(1)). The business portal (page of 29 July 2020) says no specific licence is needed and omits this.294
  • Food: each food business operator notifies every establishment for registration (Regulation (EC) No 852/2004, art. 6(2)); the Ministry of Health's form (July 2025), filed by a company with its certificate of incorporation, has an 'online sales' entry and names no fee and no time limit.295296

Selling through a marketplace

  • A platform where consumers contract with traders at a distance shows each trader's name and contacts, trade register and number, and self-certification (Regulation (EU) 2022/2065, art. 30(7)). Platform operators report sellers to the Tax Department by 31 January for the previous year; among others, a seller of fewer than 30 goods sales totalling no more than €2,000 is excluded (DAC7; Law 105(I)/2023).297298
  • Per a Green Dot news item of 15 April 2026, Temu signed agreements with Green Dot (packaging), AFIS Cyprus (batteries) and WEEE Cyprus (electrical equipment) setting up a process to help sellers on Temu meet producer-responsibility duties in Cyprus; the terms were not published. A Department of Environment officer quoted there calls those sellers producers.299

Path 4 · A shop or a café with premises

A shop needs a municipal licence; a café adds tourism and health approvals and missing use permits

In a municipality a shop needs the s. 56 premises licence; four months' silence after the town has all the papers it asks for (they differ by town) counts as a grant. It pays a yearly trade fee the Law caps. A café adds missing catering-use permits, a sanitary certificate, an approved manager and the tourism licence; alcohol and sound are licensed apart.

Terms used below: the EOA is the district local-government organisation, which since 1 July 2024 issues building permits and decides planning applications for cafés and most shops; ETEK is the Scientific and Technical Chamber: no one may practise a branch of engineering without registration in its register for that branch, its annual licence and the Greek needed, while its special-list licensees, such as licensed building technicians, may do limited work (Law 224/1990, s. 25); K.D.P. is the Cyprus numbering of regulatory administrative acts. Texts are as read on 5 and 6 October 2026.300301302303304

A shop: what applies (no document sets an order among these)

  • Use class. A building may take another use of the same class without planning permission unless a permit condition bars it; class I is 'shop', excluding hot-food shops, car showrooms and kiosks (General Development Order 2024, K.D.P. 181/2024, para. 5). ETEK and the EOAs apply the Order from 10 May 2024; the Gazette notice its para. 6 ties commencement to was not found.305
  • Change of use. Shop (class I), office (II) and café (III, 'restaurant without music, cafeteria') are different classes and nothing exempts a shop becoming a café, so a shop or office becoming a café needs planning permission, and the change of use a permit obtained in advance (Cap. 96, s. 3(1)(ε)), on the Law's wording even without works. Shops and offices pay restaurants' planning fees (K.D.P. 29/2013, para. 4).305306307
  • Premises licence. A shop, or a small café without performances, in a municipality needs a licence under s. 56 of the Municipalities Law 52(I)/2022, conforming to the planning and building laws; keeping premises without it is punishable by a fine of up to €500. Unanswered within four months of receipt of all required documents, 'the licence is deemed to have been granted' (s. 45). Papers and where to apply: below.134308
  • Enforcement. Paphos Municipality announced in December 2025 that a survey had found 'over 3,000' premises without the annual licence under ss. 55 and 56 - banks, clinics, professionals' offices and shops among them - and that from 1 March 2026 it would take legal action and immediately suspend every unlicensed premises.309
  • Trade fee. Whoever keeps or uses business premises in a municipality - an office, a shop or a café - is charged a yearly fee under s. 56(5) within ceilings in the Law, and every legal person trading there a second yearly fee under s. 56(6). The ceilings, and what is known of actual amounts, follow this list.49132
  • Signs. A 'point of sale' sign about the business carried on in a building and fixed to it needs no council approval if not across the street and within limits on height (15 feet), projection and share of the facade (a fifth) (Cap. 50, s. 6(3)); signs inside a ground-floor shop window are outside the Law. Other signs need approval, decided within four months of complete papers; silence does 'not' grant it (s. 10).310
  • Opening hours, where Part IV of the Shops Law 155(I)/2006 applies (most municipal areas and some communities): a general shop opens no earlier than 5:00 and closes by 19:30 on winter and 20:00 on summer weekdays, by 15:00 on Wednesdays, by 19:00 or 19:30 on Saturdays, and stays shut on Sundays (s. 20); December, the ten days before Easter, special shops and tourist zones fixed by decree are excepted.311312
  • Prices. Goods sold to consumers show the selling and unit price including VAT and all taxes, on the goods, packaging or shelf (Consumer Protection Law 112(I)/2021, ss. 42, 44); a shop serving consumers posts its main services' final prices inside and, where technically feasible, on the front, unless special legislation sets the sector's price information or the service cannot be priced in advance (s. 47).263313
  • Licensed goods, per the business portal (alcohol and tobacco pages last updated 7 December 2023, read 6 October 2026): alcohol and tobacco need a sale licence from the municipality or District Officer (tobacco €51.25 plus €0.85 stamp duty, valid to the next 12 March); food supplements a notification to, or for some products a licence from, the Ministry of Health.261
  • Producers. Packaging-waste costs fall on anyone who makes, imports, transports, supplies or in any way trades packaged products (Law 32(I)/2002, s. 9(1)); a Cyprus firm professionally importing electrical equipment and placing it on the market, or first supplying batteries from abroad, is a producer and enters the Environment Department's register (K.D.P. 73/2015, reg. 21; K.D.P. 125/2009; Regulation (EU) 2023/1542).271278283
  • Fire design. The designer applies Annex IV of the Streets and Buildings Regulations (K.D.P. 400/2020) to developments with no permit or application by 31 August 2020; alterations, additions or repairs to an existing licensed building at least meet its Chapters 7 (evacuation) and 9 (products). Shops need emergency lighting on escape routes, except ground-floor shops under 100 m² of sales area with exits within 15 m.314
  • Cards. A business whose main activity code is listed in Decree K.D.P. 259/2021, including restaurants and bars (56.10, 56.30) and most in-store retail (47.11-47.78), accepts cards for consumers' payments, within one month of starting if it starts after the original deadline (para. 3(δ)); any POS or virtual POS counts, a credit transfer does not; notice of card acceptance is shown at entrance and till (para. 6).69112

The s. 56 licence in plain words (Municipalities Law 52(I)/2022, s. 45, read 6 October 2026)

  • Which papers. On our reading the Law names none: the four months run from receipt of 'all the required documents' (s. 45(1)). The towns read ask different papers; Larnaca's form lists none and Nicosia's site has no s. 56 form (table below). If papers are missing, the applicant is told without delay that more are needed and how that may affect the deadline. Originals of other authorities' papers may be asked for.134308315316317318319
  • Silence. Unanswered within the four months, 'the licence is deemed to have been granted' (s. 45(2)). The Law provides no certificate of that. The one paper it makes the municipality send, as soon as possible, is an acknowledgement of receipt stating the four-month limit, the remedies against a refusal and the deemed grant (s. 45(3)); that it serves as evidence of the grant is our inference; no ruling was searched.308320
  • Extension. Section 45(1) applies 'without prejudice to' s. 13(3) of the Services Law 76(I)/2010, which lets an authority extend its deadline once, for a limited period, with reasons, before it runs out; whether a municipality may so extend its four months, neither text says expressly. That Law also wants procedures made public in advance; whether that binds the s. 56 licence for every kind of business, neither says.321322

Yearly maxima in the Law; the amount billed is the council's.

The yearly trade fee in practice

  • Who sets it. The Law caps it; the council sets the category and must publish fees left to its discretion at least a month ahead on its notice board and/or website (s. 45(5)). Premises opened, or a legal person starting, in the second half-year pay half; the premises fee may rise at most 10% a year, and the council may exempt kinds of business from it. On our reading the Fifth Schedule has no floor-area criterion.49132133134
  • Another municipality may charge a company the s. 56(6) fee only where it has a permanent place of business there or works there over 60 working days a year. On Limassol's bills, per its 2026 methodology, the s. 56(6) fee is the 'professional tax' and the s. 56(5) fee the 'charge for using business premises'.49137328
  • Getting billed. Nicosia asks a business to register for both fees on its fee-register form - name, trade name, director and ID, company number, home and premises addresses, kind of work, staff, start date, e-mail for bills - sent to invoices@nicosia.org.cy or fax 22752311. Its form for a reduced bill, on council criteria it does not print, asks evidence such as reduced turnover or a dormant company's certificate.326329
  • Past deadlines, as examples. Limassol extended its bills for professional tax, refuse and premises use to 30 December 2025 (notice of 26 November 2025); Paphos its 2025 refuse, premises and trade fees to 30 January 2026, paid only online, then 10% added; Larnaca's 2025 fees: 29 December 2025 online, 30 in person, finally 27 February 2026 at its service centres only. The Paphos and Larnaca notices give no amounts.328330331
Not there look again by 1 Feb 2027

No 2025 or 2026 per-category amounts of the s. 56(5) premises fee were found published by Nicosia, Larnaca or Paphos on 6 October 2026. Looked: Nicosia's forms, e-applications and FAQ pages and 2025-2026 minutes (22 scans unread); Larnaca's FAQ, forms and Finance pages and posts; Paphos's forms page, 2022 guide and posts. Limassol's 2026 methodology cites the Schedules without amounts; Nicosia's minutes approving its 2026 fee memo omit them. The amount is on the yearly bill, or ask the municipality's tax office.135136137

A café or restaurant adds the tourism (catering) licence of the Deputy Ministry of Tourism: a restaurant, taverna, cafeteria, bar or similar place serving food or drink for payment may not operate without it (Law 29/1985, s. 6(1)); traditional coffee-shops, take-away-only shops, certain canteens and shops with under 8 m² of service area and no alcohol are excluded (s. 2). The business portal (updated 18 September 2026) grants it only on planning and building permits for the use; for a new or altered building it orders the site owner's steps: planning permission, then the Deputy Ministry's plan approval, then the building permit.332333334

A café or restaurant: the steps, in order where a document sets one

  1. Planning permission, where the premises has none for the use
    The owner (registered or entitled to be) or his representative applies through an ETEK-licensed designer he authorises; for a company owner all directors or authorised persons sign (Planning Control Manual, January 2025). Online only, in IPPODAMOS, since 1 July 2024 (EOAs' FAQs). Cafeterias, restaurants and bars: decided by the EOA's Head of the Planning Licensing Sector (K.D.P. 350/2026), with the EOA Council's consent if the local authority objected with reasons, failing which the Council decides.302304333335336
  2. Planning fee and decisionregulations: 3 months; silence is not a grant
    Fee on submission (K.D.P. 29/2013; restaurants, catering, shops, offices): €125 per 40 m² of covered space or part, new development; €80 plus €90 per added 40 m², alterations adding space; €110, alterations without; change of use: the new use's fee. Decision or referral to the Minister notified in writing within three months of receipt or a longer period agreed in writing (K.D.P. 62/2016, reg. 6); failing it, appeal to the Council of Ministers as if refused, within 30 days after the deadline (Law 90/1972, s. 32).307337
  3. Deputy Ministry of Tourism approves the plansafter the planning permit (portal; Deputy Ministry form)
    Only for a new or altered building: the owner of the site or building - not the operator - files the plans with the planning permit on form F-KEN-01/1 (business portal, updated 18 September 2026). Nicosia's licence table (2024) also sends the planning permit to the Deputy Ministry for classification and stamping of plans before the building-permit application.334338339
  4. Building or change-of-use permitfor a new or altered building, after the plan approval (portal); Cap. 96 sets no decision time
    The owner's designer files in IPPODAMOS - for a new or altered building, with the plans the Deputy Ministry approved (portal) - and IPPODAMOS computes and collects the fee (EOA FAQs). The issuer is the EOA's Director of Development Licensing (Cap. 96, s. 2), not the municipality named in the Deputy Ministry's 2018 sheet and a Larnaca FAQ. Fire Service conditions go into the permit; cafés and shops stayed under that consultation when an Interior Ministry circular of 14 November 2024 exempted low-risk housing.301303306334336340341
  5. Works, then certificate of approvalstatute: apply within 21 days of finishing
    An owner who does not apply for the certificate of approval within 21 days of finishing gets a notice, and a fine of up to €5,000 only if he does not remedy it in time (Cap. 96, s. 21). The Fire Service's views are sought again for the inspection. No catering licence issues for premises the certificate of final approval deems unsuitable (Law 29/1985, s. 6(7)).301338340
  6. Catering manager approved (the founder if he qualifies, or a hired manager)portal: decision within 3 months of a complete application; no fee
    The manager is a graduate of a recognised hotel or tourism school with knowledge of food and drink, or has a school-leaving certificate and a year's catering experience, or three years' experience the authority accepts (classes A, B: school route only), and of good character (Law 29/1985, s. 16). Application (portal, 2 April 2021): qualifications, experience certificates, criminal record under 365 days old. The licence application carries his details; the licence issues once he is approved (portal).333334342343
  7. Sanitary certificate
    The catering licence issues on a sanitary certificate (Law 29/1985, s. 6(3)) from the Ministry of Health's service or a municipal health service, as the case may be (s. 2). The portal's catering page names the Ministry; Nicosia's guidance (2024) uses a municipal certificate on a €50 prepayment, which the Deputy Ministry also requires.300338344
  8. Apply for classification and the catering licenceportal: at least 1 month before opening; decision within 3 months of a complete application
    The operator applies before operation starts (Law 29/1985, s. 6(2)) with, per the portal (18 September 2026), the title deed, the property owner's solemn declaration, the proposed manager's details, a health certificate, the planning permit for the use and a company's Registrar certificates issued or validated within 60 days - an age limit the Deputy Ministry's own form does not set.332334338345346
  9. Licence granted; fee paidstatute: on the sanitary certificate and the fee (s. 6(3))
    The portal grants it only once the permits for the use are held and the manager approved; the 2018 procedure sheet takes the application after the works even while the building permit is pending. Fee for issue or renewal, by the class the Deputy Ministry assigns: €170 (A), €85 (B), €51 (C) in the Regulations (Law Commissioner's consolidation, archived June 2022); €170.86, €85.43 and €51.26 on the portal (18 September 2026), payable on approval.303333345
  10. Municipal licence, in a municipality: s. 55 or s. 56Law: examined within 4 months of receipt of all required documents, else deemed granted; the Law provides no certificate of that grant
    A café, restaurant or bar over 50 m², or any premises for performances or public entertainment, needs a prior s. 55 licence, on a building permit and certificates of the Municipal Engineer, Electrical and Mechanical Services (EMS), Fire Service and municipal health service. A smaller café without performances takes s. 56 on the papers its municipality asks (table above), and is told without delay of any missing. No record orders s. 55 against the catering licence; Limassol's s. 56 form wants it in most categories.134308317320324347348
  11. Alcohol retail licence (Cap. 144)statute: 2 months from complete papers, extendable once, then deemed granted
    Municipal council, or District Officer outside municipalities. Applicant 'of good character' (police certificate) and a 'fit person', premises suitable (s. 5); no legal-form, nationality or residence condition in the Law. Portal list (7 December 2023): municipal sanitary certificate and, where applicable, tourism licence and a criminal record under 3 months old. Yearly fee (portal table): €136.68 in a municipality, €85.43 elsewhere, €341.72 with entertainment by artists; half if granted after 13 September.79344349
  12. Sound emission licence, where sound may disturb nearby homesstatute: 15-day notice, 15 days for objections, issue within 1 month of a complete application
    Needed where the establishment causes or may cause noise nuisance to nearby homes and emits sound beyond its plot; issued by the municipality, or the District Officer outside them, unless a decree names another body (Law 50(I)/2016). The owner posts a 15-day notice of intent; the application carries the planning and/or building permit and the operating licence. No fee regulation is in cylaw's indexes; 2021 draft fees were still pending on 6 October 2026, per Nomoplatform.350351

A planning application for a bar, night club, beer hall, music or dance centre, discotheque and similar uses is first advertised in at least two daily newspapers in the Minister's form, and carries the signer's declaration of publication with proof; the planning authority also consults any local authority that may be affected (K.D.P. 62/2016, regs 5, 7(1)(α)). A cafeteria or restaurant without music is not named, though the authority may add any development (reg. 5(3)(ιβ)). Read 6 October 2026.352

Also for a café; no document sets an order for these

  • Outdoor tables on a pedestrian street need a licence or sub-licence of use from the municipality, 'also for the purposes of outdoor dining' (Law 52(I)/2022, s. 44(1)(ιδ), as substituted by Law 87(I)/2024); the council sets the procedure, documents, conditions (hours, space, furniture, free passage) and fee (s. 46(α)).353
  • Limassol's outdoor-dining form (October 2025) asks the building permit with permitted and current use, plans, any lease and an ID card or passport (for a company, its certificates and the director's ID or passport); Nicosia's (February 2026) permit numbers, photos, an ID-card number with no passport field and a declaration that the applicant is tenant and director. Neither states a fee or time (read 6 October 2026).354
  • Food business registration. A food business operator notifies each establishment for registration (Regulation (EC) No 852/2004, art. 6(2)); the Ministry of Health's form T-Φ-032A/7 (July 2025) goes to the district Public Health Services with a company's incorporation certificate, no fee or time named. It is silent on premises with a municipal health service; Nicosia's table (2024) lists Ministry registration once.295296339
  • Food handlers. Staff handling unpacked food hold a health certificate: after a stool test at their own cost a Chief Medical Officer issues it the same day for €22 plus a registration fee, valid 12 months (portal, 2 May 2024); in a licensed catering establishment every person employed holds a sanitary certificate (Law 29/1985, s. 17(2)).295

Who files what. The site owner, or his representative, files the planning and building-permit applications through an ETEK-licensed designer, and the plans for the Deputy Ministry's approval; that a tenant needs the landlord's authorisation is an inference, not a quoted rule. The operator applies for the catering licence; Limassol's s. 56 form asks the applicant company's certificates and the lease or use permission. Public officers issue the s. 55 certificates, and hired professionals prepare part of what they check: the EMS form needs an electrical designer and a contractor, each with an EMS register number, so a founder without one cannot sign it - an inference from the forms.304324334335336348355356

Source trap

The portal's eligibility rule is not in the Law. The business portal (18 September 2026) grants the catering licence only to persons resident in Cyprus, citizens of another EU state or legal persons situated in one, and asks a non-EU national for a residence and employment permit; Law 29/1985 (cylaw, to Law 170(I)/2020) and its Regulations (2022 archived consolidation), searched on 6 October 2026 for residence, nationality and EU terms, carry no such condition, and the portal cites no section.363364

Not there look again by 6 Jan 2027

No fee or time for the Fire Service certificate of a café over 50 m² (s. 55(2)(δ)) was found on 6 October 2026: cylaw's indexes 2021-2026 hold only staff, discipline and reserve regulations under the Fire Service Law 100(I)/2021, and the two Service pages read describe no certificate a business applies for. Per Nicosia's guidance the Service inspects by appointment and certifies only premises whose plans and conditions it approved at the planning or building-permit stage.300340365

The 2026 catering law. After the Government bill was withdrawn on 19 March 2026, a member's bill by MP Kyriakos Hadjiyiannis, 'Ο περί της Ίδρυσης και Λειτουργίας Χώρων Αναψυχής Νόμος του 2026', was passed by the plenary on 6 April 2026, per Nomoplatform's records. Per press (Dikaiosyni summarising Phileleftheros, 28 April 2026), the President referred it to the court after the Legal Service found that 'a number of its provisions' conflict with the Constitution; no Presidency statement of grounds was found on 6 October 2026.366367

Open look again by 23 Oct 2026

Status on 6 October 2026: passed, referred (per press) and not in CyLaw's 2026 list of laws; Law 29/1985 remains in force. The Supreme Constitutional Court lists presidential references by number only, so which one concerns this law is not established; its October list sets hearings of references on 8, 20 and 23 October, and no date for an opinion is published.338368369

What the 2026 law would change: the bill as tabled, and the passed text as the press reported it

  • As tabled on 26 March 2026: licensing would move from the Deputy Ministry of Tourism to the municipal or community council, merged with the council's leisure licence where both are needed; the licence would issue within two months, valid three years and renewable; Laws 1985-2020 and Regulations 1986-2005 would be repealed. The committee amended the bill before the vote; the passed text was not seen.370
  • Hours, per Ant1's report of the vote (press; passed text unpublished): catering and leisure premises and bars 7.00-2.30 Sunday-Thursday, 7.00-3.30 Friday-Saturday in May-September, to 2.00 and 3.00 in October-April; the District Officer may vary them by special licence. Tabled: 6.00-3.30 (bars 8.00-3.30) all year. Shops Law Part IV does not cover restaurants and leisure premises as to on-premises service (s. 3(2)).312371

Not established in this section

  • Fire Service certificate. What does the Fire Service charge, and how long does it take, for the certificate a café over 50 m2 needs for its municipal licence? look again by 6 Jan 2027
  • Building permit: fee and decision time. What does a building or change-of-use permit cost under the rules in force, and is there a deadline for deciding it? look again by 6 Apr 2027
  • Municipal licence and trade fees. What do municipalities actually charge for the s. 55 and s. 56 premises licences and for outdoor tables, and what yearly trade fee does each set within the Law's ceilings? look again by 1 Feb 2027
  • Sound licence: fee and term. What fee and term apply to a café's sound emission licence? look again by 6 Jan 2027
  • Catering licence term. Which term does the Deputy Ministry print on the catering licences it issues: the Law's three years, or the one year of the Regulations and the business portal? look again by 6 Jan 2027
  • The 2026 leisure-premises law. What does the law passed on 6 April 2026 say, why was it referred, and when will the court give its opinion? look again by 23 Oct 2026
  • Food-establishment registration. Does a licensed café also register with the Ministry of Health's register of food establishments, and at what cost and time? look again by 6 Jan 2027

What is known on each point, and where it was looked for, is in the register at the end.

Question 2 · Real businesses opened 2022–2026

Real cases 2022-2026: register and web dates are public; no licence issue date was found

Read from the Registrar's free search, web archives, the press and the tourism lists as of 6 October 2026. Two of the barest businesses were public before their registration; the tourism lists seen print at most a licence's expiry, no issue date; B-READY 2025's 11.5 days for company entry is an expert median, not a case.

Each case is assembled from the Registrar of Companies' free search as served on 6 October 2026, web-archive captures, the businesses' own websites, Wolt's trader panels, the EU's VIES service, the Register of Social Enterprises, the press and the Deputy Ministry of Tourism's lists of catering and entertainment venues. Elapsed times are calendar days between two named dates, start day excluded, counted for this guide, not published. Founders' own accounts are labelled; none is verified.67297372373374375376377378

Source trap

The Valiotti account describes 2022 rules. A Council of Ministers decision of 30 November 2022, in force 12 December 2022, added proof of an initial investment of at least EUR 200,000 to registration as a company of foreign interests; since 30 June 2026 applications go only through the company's CY Login profile. The Business Facilitation Unit's published time went from 'within seven (7) working days' (page of May 2022) to 'in no more than ten (10) business days' (page modified 23 September 2026).387389390
