How to read the register

  • The free search shows number, type, status, registration date, directors and secretary (or a business name's owner), address, 'pending services' with application dates, the date the last HE32 was registered and lodged HE32s with made-up dates; no shareholders (6 October 2026). Monthly open-data files on data.gov.cy were last modified 29 September 2026.372
  • Registration dates include weekends: in the Registrar's open-data file of 29 September 2026, 2,647 of 16,780 companies registered in 2025 (15.8%) carry a Saturday or Sunday date, against 6.5% for 2023 and 9.2% for 2024 (a count made for this guide, companies since struck off included); so elapsed times are counted in calendar days.412
  • Every company with share capital delivers its annual return within 28 days of the date it is made up to (Companies Law s. 120(1)); the first reference date is the day after 18 months from incorporation (s. 120(1A)(a)). Applications came 61 days after the made-up date at KFB Hospitality Concepts Ltd, 221 and 77 days at N.N. Cyber Digital Products Ltd, and 293 days at Kuns Studio Ltd (not shown here).45394397
  • The free search cannot find business names by their owner: names held under other names cannot be searched by owner. It returns at most 200 results per search, and no details page opens outside a search (6 October 2026).372411
Source trap

A pending HE32 is a lodged return, not a registered one. On 6 October 2026 nine HE32 applications of five companies followed in the research (the three above, plus Kuns Studio Ltd and J.P Mojo Design Limited, whose application of 17 November 2023 is the oldest, 1,054 days) sit both under 'pending services' and in the HE32 archive, with the date of the last HE32 registration empty; in a spot check of eight unrelated companies with any HE32, seven show the same. The search does not say what 'pending' means.385413414

How to read the tourism lists

  • The Deputy Ministry of Tourism publishes one list per district, on gov.cy (Greek, dated 19/06/2026) and visitcyprus.com (English editions, the newest seen a Nicosia list dated 11/08/2026): trade name, category, address, mostly a telephone, and in Greek editions from 06/11/2025 a licence-expiry column, blank for some rows. None seen on 6 October 2026 names a holder or gives a licence number or issue date.374415
  • Older editions sit on visitcyprus.com, newer ones on gov.cy. The Nicosia list of 06/11/2025 survives only as a web-archive copy of 14 November 2025, its gov.cy address answering 'not found' (HTTP 404) on 6 October 2026; the Limassol list of that date also survives as a web-archive copy of a gov.cy file.415416
Source trap

A list date bounds a change, it does not date it. Each café's name replaced an entry listed at that address for at least two and a half years, and two of the three new entries kept the earlier venue's telephone numbers. The lists do not say whether an entry was renamed, transferred or newly issued.406408417

An aggregate, not a case: the World Bank's B-READY 2025, the first edition to cover Cyprus (report of 29 December 2025), gives 11.5 calendar days for 'the entire company entry process', domestic and foreign-owned alike, at 1,920 euro (domestic) and 1,750 (foreign). These are medians of expert answers as of 1 September 2024 for the most common limited company in the largest city, with employees and turnover above the VAT threshold, not register measurements; cost is official fees plus the experts' estimate where a step has none, VAT excluded, a lawyer only if mandatory.253254

B-READY 2025, Cyprus: per-step medians of expert answers

StepMedian timeMedian cost (euro)
Name verification3 days30255
Beneficial-owner registration1 day0255
Third-party intermediaries, required by law per the experts4 days1,450 (experts' estimate)255258259
Registration at the registry6 days575255
Tax registration2.5 days0255
Employer or employee registration4 days0255
Published total11.5 days1,920 domestic; 1,750 foreign253255

Reproducibility package, files of 14 April 2026. No bank account or general licence is counted; some steps are marked as done at the same time.

Source trap

The published total is not the sum of the steps: each figure is a separate median, so the per-step medians sum to 20.5 days and 2,055, and the questionnaire's own sum-of-steps field gives 21.5 days and 1,885. For a foreign-owned company the experts report no additional or differing step, yet the published foreign total is 1,750.256

What the B-READY figures leave out

  • The bank account: the experts' median answer to whether opening one is required by law or commonly done in practice to start a domestic company is No, so it is not in the 11.5 days and 1,920. The questionnaire counts the step only where needed for entry, such as depositing paid-in minimum capital, and says to answer No where an account is opened only for operational reasons.257
  • Per-step data from 2026: the Methodology Handbook's Third Edition (January 2026) asks only for total time and cost, so B-READY 2026 data can carry no per-step time for Cyprus.418
  • Foreign owners: the experts' answer that foreign entrepreneurs face no screening different from domestic ones (reference point 1 September 2024) predates Law 194(I)/2025, in force since 2 April 2026, which requires approval of some non-EU investments.259

Not established in this section

  • Founders' own dated accounts. How long did each step take, in the founders' own words, for businesses registered in 2022-2026? look again by 6 Apr 2027
  • Licence dates of premises cases. When were the case cafés and shops licensed? look again by 6 Apr 2027

What is known on each point, and where it was looked for, is in the register at the end.

Question 3 · Who to hire, what they close, what it costs

Only an advocate may draft a company's founding documents; later routine filings name no lawyer

Cyprus law reserves drafting a company's memorandum and articles to a practising advocate, audits to licensed auditors, plans to registered engineers and architects, and director, secretary and office services offered to others to eligible persons. Firms publish formation prices on different bases as of 6 October 2026; none is an official fee.

Acts the law reserves, and what the founder may do himself

ActWho may do it, and the instrumentThe founder himself
Drafting the memorandum and articles, or a resolution on the company's organisationA practising advocate only (Advocates Law, Cap. 2, ss. 2(1), 11(1)); a memorandum drafted otherwise is void (s. 11(5)). Unlicensed practice (s. 11(3)) and undertaking or advertising to draft one (s. 11(5)(ε)) are offences.No. He signs before a witness, for whom neither the Companies Law nor the Registrar's forms set a qualification.39419420421422
Declaration of compliance, form HE1 (the incorporation declaration)By the Companies Law (Cap. 113, s. 17(2)), the formation advocate or a person named in the articles as director or secretary; the Registrar's form is worded only for the advocate.By Cap. 113, s. 17(2), yes, if so named, and s. 37A(4) lets the declaration be filed electronically. But the Registrar's incorporation page asks on both routes for HE1 sworn by the entrusted lawyer; no Registrar announcement of September 2003 to 30 September 2026 says a director may make it, and firms disagree.40221228229
Audit or review; books; tax computationsAudit or review: a licensed statutory auditor or audit firm (Law 53(I)/2017, s. 33(1); Cap. 113, s. 152A(1)(d)). From 1 January 2026 the Tax Commissioner may disregard tax computations not certified by an ICPAC (Institute of Certified Public Accountants) practising-certificate holder or other recognised body (Law 4/1978, s. 29).Not the audit or review. Cap. 113 puts the books on the directors and no provision of it requires a licensed accountant (s. 141(1)). A sole trader with turnover and other gross income up to €120,000 is exempt from accounts that must be audited or reviewed (Law 4/1978, art. 30, from tax year 2026); he still keeps books.11423424425

Every company keeps a registered office in Cyprus (Cap. 113, s. 102); whether a founder giving his own address 'provides' it, the Act does not say.Exempt as director if he or his family own 25%+, or, not controlling its board, of under ten companies; if resident, as secretary if they own 50%+ (25% if all shareholders are Cyprus-resident individuals) or it is his sole employer; only if not advertised or offered to others (s. 4(3)). None covers an address.232234235236
Engineering studies and plans; planning applicationA person on ETEK's register for the branch, with ETEK's annual licence and the Greek needed (ETEK, the Scientific and Technical Chamber: Law 224/1990, s. 25(1)). Statutes as consolidated on cylaw, read 4-6 October 2026. A sole director may be secretary only in a single-member company (Cap. 113, s. 171(1)).

Open look again by 6 Apr 2027

Prescribed, not in force on 6 October 2026: K.D.P. 368/2018 sets a new form HE1 whose compliance declaration is made only by 'a practising advocate' entrusted with the formation, with his Bar register number; a proposed director or secretary signs a separate declaration as 'authorised to submit this form'. It applies only once the Registrar brings it into force by Gazette notice; the 2021 notices do not cover HE1 and cylaw's indexes to 18 September 2026 show no later one.226

What the founder can file himself, and where (Registrar's and Tax Department's texts, October 2026)

  • Name approval and online incorporation: the Registrar's e-filing, entered with a CY Login account. Any registered user may submit, but the file carries HE1 sworn by the entrusted lawyer. €10 a name; €165 to register a company with share capital (€235 without).38157220
  • A business name, after name approval: form EE1 by hand or by post, or online as a registered e-filing user with a witness's solemn declaration of signatures, plus any authority's consent the name or the business requires; €80 (+€40 accelerated), within one month of starting business. The guidance asks for no advocate's declaration and no memorandum.24426
  • Changes of directors or secretary (HE4) and share-transfer notices (HE57): within 14 days, online or by hand or post, €20 each (+€20 accelerated); the guidance names no lawyer. The annual return (HE32): e-filing only, with the previous year's financial statements, €20 (+€20 accelerated); its review criteria ask for an officer's signature.45222421
  • Beneficial owners (UBO): per the Registrar's guidance, filed free, only by the company's own officer or partner, not by a service provider unless itself an officer; the Directive (K.D.P. 112/2021) asks only electronic filing with access codes the entity obtains. A director without a Cypriot identity card cannot use his own CY Login; the company's profile, verified in person, or a legal-person officer's remains.33241
  • Tax registration of a company: only through the Tax For All (TFA) portal with CY Login, since 1 July 2026. VAT registration: printed form T.F. 1101 at a District Tax Office or, per the business portal, at the Point of Single Contact. Filing VAT returns for another needs a TFA 04 'representative' listing, no professional qualification.95225427

The advocates' stamp is an Advocates' Pension Fund fee under Regulations made under s. 27 of the Advocates Law. Regulation 3(3), inserted by K.D.P. 103/1988 (K.D.P.: the Cyprus numbering of regulatory administrative acts) and not rewritten since, charges it 'for the filing by an advocate' with the Registrar. The Table (K.D.P. 605/2007, from 1 January 2008; these items not changed by the 2017-2023 amendments) sets €40 on the founding document of a limited company with capital up to €2,000, €26 for a partnership, and €9 on a business-name application, HE32, HE2 (change of registered office) and HE4.159160428429430431432433

Regulation 3(3) charges the stamp on filings by an advocate and names no fee on these forms filed by the founder or an officer himself. Of the Registrar's review-criteria pages (2019), only the company-registration page names a stamp, on HE1, listing a missing stamp first among the 'most common reasons for rejection'; the criteria for HE2, HE4, HE32 and EE1 name none, and those forms have no stamp field.160222

Open look again by 6 Jan 2027

Not tested as of 6 October 2026: whether the Registrar's e-filing system charges the stamp on, or refuses, an HE32, HE2, HE4 or business-name application the founder files himself. The Regulations, review criteria and forms read do not show it, and no Registrar announcement of September 2003 to 30 September 2026 deals with stamps on self-filed forms. How the stamp is collected on an e-filed incorporation was not established either.160222229434

Source trap

By statute the stamp is the advocate's own cost: Cap. 2, s. 29(1) forbids him to charge a client any fee he paid under s. 27(1). Firms still list €49 for it at €1,000 capital: George K. Konstantinou LLC (page of 3 October 2026), EasyCorporate ('€49 (as of January 2026)', guide of 13 March 2026), Savva Cyprus and Expanship (July 2026). The scale gives €40; no Part III item is €49; no Bar circular setting €49 was found on 4 October 2026 (Bar guide for new advocates, Pension Fund site, cylaw).435436437438

Each supervisor keeps its own register (Law 196(I)/2012, s. 25(2)); CySEC only links to the other two.

Hiring in writing, and complaining

  • Lawyers have had no minimum fees for out-of-court work since 29 June 2018, when K.D.P. 172/2018 repealed the minimum-fee regulations of 1985-2017. Under K.D.P. 171/2018 an advocate must tell the client in advance how the fee will be set, 'especially' when hourly, and deliver a fee account within a reasonable time and at the latest one month after the client asks; breach is a disciplinary offence.479
  • The written appointment in an out-of-court matter: an 'Advocate Appointment Form' or agreement the client signs, stating the matter, the agreed fee and how it is paid (Annex I to K.D.P. 171/2018), releases the advocate from the regulations' reasonable-fee criteria. An advocate's licence is issued or renewed only with professional-negligence insurance (Cap. 2, s. 6E(1)).480481
  • A lawyer's conduct: a written complaint to the Advocates' Disciplinary Board, €68 fee, for fraudulent, disgraceful or unprofessional conduct or breach of the conduct rules; mishandling a case is not itself an offence. A private complaint proceeds only with the Board's leave, and if the advocate is acquitted the complainant may be ordered to pay costs (Cap. 2, s. 17).482483
  • An accountant: ICPAC, only for those on its registers, filed on its website with a €200 fee; it imposes disciplinary penalties only and leaves compensation to the courts. An auditor or audit work: the Cyprus Public Audit Oversight Board.484
  • A fee dispute in an out-of-court matter: a Bar committee of three advocates under K.D.P. 171/2018, whose decisions have the status of arbitral awards enforceable through a District Court.482
Source trap

The Bar's public page 'Inadequate Professional Services', read 6 October 2026, still gives the Disciplinary Board's fine limit as one thousand pounds. Cap. 2, s. 17 (cylaw text through Law 17(I)/2024) lets the Board warn or reprimand an advocate, fine him up to €20,000, suspend his licence or strike him off; costs ordered against a complainant are assessed on the €10,000-€50,000 scale of civil actions.483

  • Own filings and 'practising law'. Does a company's own officer who prepares its filings 'practise law' under the Advocates Law? look again by 6 Apr 2027
  • Price of licence and permit work. What do firms charge to prepare a café's licence applications or the change-of-use and building-permit work? look again by 6 Jan 2027
  • Self-employed bookkeeping package. Does any regulated accounting firm publish a dated package of bookkeeping plus tax return for the self-employed? look again by 6 Jan 2027

The foreign element · When the owner is foreign

A foreign founder files the same company forms; permits, papers, screening and banks add the rest

The Registrar's criteria identify founders by passport or ID number and ask for no residence permit; per an agency report, she seeks other services' views on companies with Turkish or Turkish Cypriot officers. The foreign element shows mainly in the right to run a business, investor permits, papers from abroad, branches, screening, land, sanctions and banks.

The Registrar's review criteria (read 5 October 2026) identify an individual director or secretary by 'identity or passport number, country of origin, date of birth and profession', and a shareholder by address, identity number, nationality and profession. No residence permit, registration certificate or Cyprus address of the person is asked for; only the registered office must be in Cyprus. The wording is the same for an EU citizen and a third-country national. An outline assembled from the Companies Law and the immigration texts read on 5 October 2026 found no approval needed to hold shares or to be a director; what is regulated is activity.233485

Source trap

Per a Cyprus News Agency report, the Registrar told a House committee on 3 July 2025 that she asks other services for views on companies with Turkish nationals or Turkish Cypriots among their officers. For 2023 to mid-2025: with Turkish nationals 30 registered, 7 rejected, 21 withdrawn 'mainly because of the delay' in the reply, 13 pending; with Turkish Cypriots 41 registered, 10 rejected, 16 pending; 20 more with other third-country persons rejected.486

Who may run a business: residence statuses by name (in depth on the Cyprus banking page)

  • EU or EEA citizen: per the Migration Department, Union citizens 'enjoy free access to employment, including employed or self-employed activity'. One staying over three months registers within four months of arrival (€20), but the certificate may 'in no case' be made a precondition where entitlement can be shown by other means (Law 7(I)/2007, arts 10, 23(1)).180181
  • Third-country national: long-term residents; family of an EU citizen or a Blue Card holder; a Cypriot's spouse or partner, or a minor Cypriot's parent (policy, after a residence and employment permit); those under international or (on conditions) temporary protection; Startup Visa founders in their company (scheme stated valid to December 2026); immigration permit holders (A-D); Regulation 12 permit holders.3192193194195196197199
  • Not enough: a visitor's permit; a permit naming an employer (work there only); the Blue Card; the Digital Nomad permit (no Cyprus employers or clients). Pupil's, student's, visitor's, employment and other special permit holders may not seek the Regulation 12 permit in Cyprus. Investor permit (policy): employment only as director of the company invested in; if not invested in shares, shareholder and unpaid director.56189190195203487
  • A third-country national who finished studies in Cyprus at master's level or above may stay twelve months 'to seek employment or set up a business' (Law 7(I)/2019, art. 25), applying at least 30 days before the study permit expires; after three months proof of a genuine chance may be asked.202

The investment residence route, the press's 'golden visa', is an immigration permit giving permanent residence, granted under Regulation 6(2) of the Aliens and Immigration Regulations outside their Regulation 5 categories. It rests on that power and a published policy, not a law: the investor criteria on the Migration Department's page, 4th revision, in force 2 May 2023. Per that page (read 6 October 2026), the right is 'of unlimited validity' for holder and adult dependants; the card expires ten years after issue and must be replaced; minors' permits run to age 18. The page speaks of residence only and says nothing about citizenship.488489490

The investor permit, Regulation 6(2), as read 6 October 2026

TopicWhat the Department's texts say
Investment: €300,000 in one of four(A) a new home from a developer, €300,000 plus VAT, up to two units (resales only on contracts filed before 7 May 2013); (B) other real estate (offices, shops, hotels), resales too; (C) share capital of a Cyprus company operating there with at least five staff; (D) units of Cyprus AIF, AIFLNP or RAIF funds investing in Cyprus.491
Money from abroadShown transferred to Cyprus from abroad, from the applicant's or a dependant spouse's account or, per a clarification of 17 May 2023, a company solely theirs whose shareholder is on the application; paid to the seller's account in Cyprus; for real estate, not domestic borrowing. Buying through a company they solely own counts.492
IncomeSecure income of at least €50,000 a year, plus €15,000 for a spouse and €10,000 per dependent minor child; the spouse's income counts. For a new home (A) it must come from abroad; for B-D, partly or wholly from Cyprus too. Proof: a tax return of the country of tax residence or an independent certified accountant's certificates.493
Criminal record, health, housingApplicant and spouse: clean criminal records from the country of origin and, if different, of residence; no threat to public order or security. Applicant and dependants: inpatient and outpatient health insurance. For B-D, or a new home with too few bedrooms: proof of a home in Cyprus (title, paid sale contract or lease).494
FamilyCovers the spouse and children under 18. Unmarried dependent students of 18-25 abroad may apply themselves, with €10,000 more parental income each. Adult children not financially dependent need a larger investment (per the policy's examples, €600,000 with one, €900,000 with two) and €50,000 income each. No provision for parents.495496
WorkApplicant and spouse sign that they will take no employment in Cyprus except as directors of the company they invested in. If the investment is not in a company's share capital, they may be shareholders of Cyprus companies (dividends are no obstacle) and unpaid directors of them.487494
ApplyingForm MIP2 (box 'Regulation 6(2) - Investment Criteria'), on the Department's investor forms page, is filed at the Migration Department's head office, 90 Makarios III Avenue, Nicosia, in person or by an authorised representative, 'after arranging an appointment or any other way decided'.497
Fee and papers€500, plus €70 per person for an Alien Registration Certificate where none exists. Foreign documents apostilled and in Greek or English or officially translated. An applicant in Cyprus attends for photograph, fingerprints and signature; one abroad gives them before the card is issued.497
TimeHandled 'under an expedited procedure'; with all criteria met and no criminal-record, public-order or security concern, it goes to the Deputy Minister to decide. 'Approximately two (2) months' from a completed application: an estimate, not a legal limit. No actual times found on the Department's policy, forms or news pages.498
After the grantYearly, holders prove the investment is kept and, if no longer under GeSY (the General Healthcare System), health insurance; every three years holder and adult family file clean criminal records. Cards are renewed on form MIP2 from three months before expiry (passport copy, old card, €50), outside the July 2026 online service.8499500
How it is lostCancelled if the investment is disposed of without at once replacing it with an equal or larger one meeting the criteria, or the required proofs fail. It ceases if holder and dependants abroad do not take up residence in Cyprus within a year of approval, or if they acquire permanent residence abroad or are absent two years.489499
Source trap

VAT in the €300,000 paid for real estate: the Migration Department's own texts disagree (read 6 October 2026). The policy (para 4.1) asks for payment receipts of 'at least €300,000 (excluding VAT)'; its required-documents list asks, for categories A and B, 'proof of payment for at least €300.000 (plus VAT)', and a notice of 19 March 2026 on dependent students asks for 'at least €300,000 plus VAT'. No text reconciling them was found on the Department's policy, forms or news pages.501

Source trap

Share capital or share purchase: for the company route the policy describes €300,000 put into the share capital of a newly registered company, or a €300,000 increase of an existing company's capital. The Department's required-documents list instead opens with a 'Share Purchase Agreement of a minimum value of €300.000', wording that, on our reading, also fits buying existing shares from another holder, carried over from the 2021 revision. No text reconciling the two was found (6 October 2026).502

What the investor permit is not

  • Not citizenship. The investment citizenship programme naturalised investors 'by exception'. Per 2016 Ministry guidelines, a Cabinet decision had them first hold a residence permit; those without could seek a 6(2) permit alongside, 'solely' for that purpose, cancelled on naturalisation or refusal. The programme ended on 1 November 2020; Law 224(I)/2025 deleted its legal basis. A 6(2) permit alone gives residence only.503504505
  • Not a 'company of foreign interests', which the Business Facilitation Unit registers on a €200,000 initial investment and which may employ third-country staff, directors among them, on temporary residence and employment permits. Read together, the texts make it a status of a company, the investor permit one of a person, neither a condition of the other; none says if the company's owner may hold such a staff permit.506507508

How the criteria moved, 2016-2026

SinceWasNowActWeight
24 Mar 20212nd revision, 22 February 2016: new real estate only, €300,000 excluding VAT, €200,000 paid; €30,000 three-year deposit; income from abroad €30,000 plus €5,000 a dependant, €8,000 a dependent parent; parents' permits; no employment at all.Four €300,000 options: new home, other real estate (resales too), share capital of a Cyprus company with five staff, Cyprus fund units; no deposit. Applicant and spouse may be directors of the company invested in or, if not investing in shares, unpaid directors.3rd revision (archived)509510High · foreign owner · easesIt opened company shares and fund units as investments and let investor and spouse be directors of the company invested in, where the 2016 terms took real estate only and allowed no employment.
2 May 2023Income €30,000, plus €5,000 per dependant and €8,000 per dependent parent; parents' permits; €200,000 paid on real estate at application; criminal record from the country of residence (or Cyprus).Income €50,000, plus €15,000 per spouse and €10,000 per minor child; no parents' permits; €300,000 paid; criminal records from the countries of origin and of residence; health insurance and yearly monitoring added.4th revision; per the Ministry, to remove weaknesses that 'might have left room for its exploitation'510511512High · foreign owner · adds burdenEvery new applicant meets the higher income floor (€30,000 to €50,000) and, once granted, a yearly proof; parents can no longer get permits.

Per press: in April 2026 AKEL MPs proposed amending the Aliens and Immigration Law so the scheme continues only under House-approved regulations; the House interior committee closed its discussion on 15 April. No outcome was found; CyLaw shows no 2026 amendment of that Law. On 24 September 2026 the Deputy Migration Minister told MPs that decisions on tightening are expected and he had sent proposals to other ministers. No text, Cabinet decision or date was found on gov.cy or in the press by 6 October 2026.515516

Source trap

To verify a company's CY Login profile an officer signs form CYL/1, which takes an identity, ARC or passport number, and hands it in or names on it another person to do so; a provider may file CYL/3 with the officer's CYL/2. The help page asks for the 'civil identity card' of whoever presents the papers and names no other document for a non-Cypriot (read 5 October 2026).237238

Papers from abroad

  • Translation: foreign-language official documents enter public registers after translation by a sworn translator (Law 45(I)/2019, s. 18); the Registrar's pages also accept a Greek translation 'by an affidavit in court'. Since 25 July 2025 Cap. 113 (ss. 365ΙΑ-365ΙΒ) has the Registrar issue, on application, English-language certificates or certified copies admissible in any legal proceedings as equal to the original.524525
  • Certification: the Bar's AML Directive (August 2023) gives advocates a 'suggested (non-exhaustive)' list for a corporate client, Cypriot or foreign, including evidence of good standing, certified by a notary, a Hague apostille, a regulated professional in the EU or an equivalent or FATF state, or a Cyprus embassy or consulate.526
  • Directive (EU) 2025/25 (in force since 30 January 2025) has national measures apply from 31 July 2028: an EU Company Certificate 'accepted in all Member States as sufficient evidence' of incorporation, and register-certified copies freed from legalisation, apostille included. Transposition is due by 31 July 2027; on 6 October 2026 no Cyprus law was found (CyLaw's 2025-2026 lists; EUR-Lex: 0 measures).527528529

Directors and the company's tax residence

  • From 1 January 2026 a company is Cyprus tax resident if managed and controlled in Cyprus or incorporated there, unless a tax treaty provides otherwise (Law 244(I)/2025). Form T.D.98 asks whether most board meetings are held, key decisions taken and minutes kept in Cyprus, and most directors are tax resident there. Certificates: treaty countries only; the page does not say what a company incorporated abroad submits.530531532
  • Under the Estonia-Cyprus convention, a company resident in both states under their laws meets no automatic tie-breaker: the authorities settle it 'by mutual agreement', with regard to the place of effective management or any other relevant criterion; Estonia's reservation on MLI Article 4 leaves this in place.533

A branch of a foreign company

  • A foreign company files within a month of setting up a place of business (Cap. 113, s. 347(1)), naming a Cyprus resident to accept service; forms AE1-AE3 cost €380, plus €100 accelerated (6 October 2026). Guidance: true copies of the constitution, apostilled (non-Hague countries: competent authority), translated into Greek unless in Greek or English (the criteria omit the exception). No maximum age is stated.534535
  • A branch is not a new legal entity. It files home and branch accounts yearly, subject to exemptions (s. 350). Managed and controlled abroad, the company is taxed as a non-resident on the branch's profits at 15% and 'may elect' resident treatment; managed and controlled in Cyprus, it is resident. No charge on remitting branch profits was found in the income tax or defence contribution laws.536537538
  • By statute, online registration of an overseas company's branch is completed within 10 working days (Cap. 113, s. 366Η(6), inserted by Law 151(I)/2023).42
  • The Registrar's page 'After the establishment of place of business' (read 6 October 2026, citing no law) gives an overseas company sixty days from registration to register with the Tax Department. Law 4/1978, s. 5A(2), sets 60 days from a company's incorporation or registration in Cyprus from 1 January 2026; since 1 July 2026 overseas companies (AE numbers) apply only through CY Login.43539540
Source trap

Two Registrar pages on overseas companies still ask a €350 annual fee by 30 June, while its companies' page says the levy was repealed from 2024 by Law 25(I)/2024, which deleted s. 391, Cap. 113's only annual-fee provision (read 6 October 2026).143536

Investment screening and land

  • Since 2 April 2026 (Law 194(I)/2025) the Finance Ministry must first approve a deal where a foreign investor (not an EU, EEA or Swiss national, or a third-country undertaking; residence not mentioned) takes 25% or more, worth €2,000,000 or more with the same parties' 12-month deals, in a strategic-importance target; crossing 25% or 50% in such a target needs notice at any value. Not notifying: €5,000-50,000 fine.541542543544
  • A greenfield investment does 'not satisfy the statutory conditions for mandatory notification', though it may be examined within 15 months of completion where security or public order may be affected; strategic importance turns on 'the actual activities' in Cyprus (guidance, 28 July 2026). B-READY 2025's answer of no separate screening of foreign entrepreneurs (reference date 1 September 2024) predates the Law.259545546
  • Under Cap. 109 an 'alien' needs the Council of Ministers' prior permission to acquire immovable property other than on death. Aliens include non-citizens, foreign companies and companies where aliens hold half or more of directors, votes, shares or control; not EU or EEA citizens, legal persons of another EU state seated in the EEA, 'alien Cypriots' or a citizen's non-citizen wife not separated by court decision.547548
  • Acquisition includes a lease over 33 years (or extendable past it by unilateral options), or over ten years for land beyond a home's or premises' needs and in any case over two donums, and a share making a property-holding Cyprus company alien-controlled; on the text an ordinary commercial tenancy needs no permission. District Administration: form COMM 145, free, 2-3 weeks (Ministry of Interior, 2 October 2024).549550
  • Per a Cyprus News Agency report, four members' bills to tighten Cap. 109 were pending before the House Interior Committee on 3 September 2026 (texts not read); the plenary vote on three of them had been postponed on 6 and 16 April 2026 (Nomoplatform). The committee chair said it would return to the subject in October 2026, including rules for companies; no session date was found by 6 October 2026.551552553
  • Per the Ministry of Interior (2 October 2024), a foreign national or couple may be allowed a plot of up to 4,000 m² for an own home, or up to two units: two dwellings, a dwelling and a shop of up to 100 m², or a dwelling and an office of up to 250 m². Under Cap. 109, s. 3(1A), land beyond a home's or premises' strict needs 'and in any case' over two σκάλες meets further conditions; Lands Department: 2,676 m².550554
Source trap

Cap. 109's 'trust for an alien' reads differently in the Greek consolidated text and the English one of 2007: the English excludes leases of 33 years or less, the Greek, read literally, does not clearly do so. Per the English edition only the Gazette texts are authentic; the 1972 Gazette original was not read.555

Sanctions

  • Regulation (EU) No 833/2014 bars, since 24 October 2025, crypto-asset services, payment instruments, acquiring, payment initiation and e-money for Russian nationals, residents and entities (art. 5b(2)), and since 26 February 2022 deposits over €100,000 per bank from them or non-EU entities over 50% owned by them (art. 5b(1)). EU, EEA and Swiss nationals and residence-permit holders are exempt.556557558
  • Since 27 March 2023 Russian nationals and residents may not sit on governing bodies of owners or operators of critical infrastructures and critical entities (art. 5o), only EU, EEA and Swiss nationals exempt; not a general bar on Russians directing EU companies. Violations are crimes (Law 149(I)/2025), and under the Central Bank's 2020 directive banks screen directors, owners and signatories before any service.559560561
  • Trusts and similar arrangements whose trustor or beneficiary is a Russian national, resident or entity may not be registered or given an address or management services (since 9 April 2022), and since 5 July 2022 no one may act as their trustee, nominee shareholder, director or secretary (art. 5m of Regulation (EU) No 833/2014). EU, EEA and Swiss nationals and residence-permit holders are exempt.562
  • Since 25 August 2026 Russian nationals and residents of Russia may not own or control, or sit on the governing bodies of, an EU legal person providing crypto-asset services (art. 5b(2a), as replaced by Regulation (EU) 2026/1848); from 18 January 2024 the bar covered only wallet, account and custody providers. EU, EEA and Swiss nationals and residence-permit holders there are exempt.563

In the texts read on 4-6 October 2026 no maximum age for a foreign parent's certificates is published: the Central Bank's directive sets none, Bank of Cyprus's 2017 form asks good standing only of foreign companies over two years old, and JCC's form v2.2 (the current v2.3 could not be read on 6 October) and Stripe give only address-proof and 'current and not expired' rules.565568570571

Not established in this section

  • Tax residence certificate for a foreign company. What does a foreign company with a Cyprus branch, or one incorporated abroad and managed and controlled in Cyprus, file for a Cyprus tax residence certificate? look again by 6 Jan 2027
  • Age of a foreign company's documents. How old may a foreign parent company's certificates be when a Cyprus bank, acquirer or advocate receives them? look again by 6 Jan 2027
  • EU electronic identities at the Registrar. Which EU electronic identities does the Registrar accept for online procedures, and can an EU founder file with his home eID today? look again by 6 Jan 2027

What is known on each point, and where it was looked for, is in the register at the end.

Why · The reasons behind the changes

Why rules changed: reasons sit in bills, committee reports and speeches; some changes state none

Stated reasons come from Government reports to the House, MPs' memoranda, committee reports, a Minister's speech and the Fiscal Council; press accounts fill gaps. The 15% corporate rate has two accounts. The 2026 strike-off drive, the 2018 shell-company circular and the minimum-wage decree state no reason.

Tax

Change and dateAct and statusReason as the record states it, and whoseWeight
Corporate tax 12.5% → 15%, 1 January 2026Law 244(I)/2025; in forceFinance Minister, budget speech (4 December 2025): the Government's 'political will to strengthen the international credibility and reputation of our country', 'in alignment with international standards', without harm to competitiveness; no figure.89573High · company · adds burdenRaises the rate on company profits from 12.5% to 15% from 1 January 2026: a tax rate every company with taxable profit meets.
The same rise: a second accountNote of the Fiscal Council, an independent body, August 2025It 'should be regarded as exogenously given' and 'largely abolishes Cyprus's tax competition with other destinations', yet 'cannot be judged undesirable': it answers 'pressures and risks', threats 'related also to the country's reputation' and the 'adverse selection' inherent in foreign direct investment in Cyprus.574High · company · adds burdenThe same rise, 12.5% to 15%, rated the same; the Fiscal Council says it largely abolishes Cyprus's tax competition with other destinations.
Deemed dividend distribution ended for 2026 profits; defence contribution on dividends of resident, domiciled individuals 17% → 5%, 1 January 2026Law 245(I)/2025; in force, with transitional rulesFinance Minister, same speech: to 'correct distortions of the tax system' and encourage reinvestment of profits in productive investment.90573575High · company · easesA rate change on profit taken out: the defence contribution on dividends of resident, domiciled owners falls from 17% to 5%, and 2026 profits are no longer deemed distributed; older profits keep transitional rules.
The reform as a whole, 1 January 2026Six tax laws, Gazette of 31 December 2025; in forcePreamble of Laws 244(I) and 245(I)/2025: a fairer, proportional system, competitiveness, effective collection. Ministry of Finance report (30 October 2025): last broad reform in 2002, the environment 'has changed radically'; the bill includes provisions from a study under recovery-plan milestone 211.576577578579High · own name, company · mixedSix tax laws took effect on 1 January 2026 (a few provisions later), among them income tax, defence contribution and tax assessment and collection; the Ministry expected a neutral effect on businesses on average.
Company tax registration within 60 days; the Commissioner may suspend a business and seal its premises for up to ten days on listed grounds, 1 January 2026Law 243(I)/2025; in forceThe records carry no stated reason for these provisions. The House Finance Committee report (18 December 2025) records reservations, not saying whose, on the new powers; revised texts of 9 and 12 December lowered proposed penalties and gave a company 60 days from incorporation to register.43580581Medium · own name, company · adds burdenA 60-day deadline for a company's tax registration and a new sanction, suspension and sealing for up to ten days, for listed failures such as not issuing receipts; no new tax.
Stamp duty repealed in full, 1 January 2026Law 239(I)/2025; in forceA House initiative: the Government proposed only a partial end. Business and professional bodies called it 'a complicated system that does not reflect modern practices' (Finance Committee report, 18 December 2025); the repeal bill's memorandum states only its purpose.582583Medium · own name, company · easesRemoves State stamp duty on documents, including those filed at the Registrar; the records give no amount, and the Bar's stamp on form HE1 stays.
Incorporation test for company tax residence, 31 December 2022Law 193(I)/2021; reworded from 2026EU recovery-plan milestone 209 (Council annex, July 2021), in a reform whose stated objective is a fair tax system 'by combatting tax evasion and aggressive tax planning by Multinational Enterprises'.584585Medium · company, foreign owner · adds burdenReaches only companies incorporated in Cyprus but managed and controlled abroad and not tax resident elsewhere: they become Cyprus tax resident.

The Registrar objected (the charges brought 'high rates of compliance'); the Ministries asked to raise the proposed €2,000 cap to €5,000 and add strike-off, as the law did.600601602Medium · company · mixedLowers the penalty for not filing beneficial owners (cap €20,000 to €5,000) but lets the Registrar strike off non-filers; it reaches only companies that fail to file.
Register closed to the public: suspended 23 November 2022; legitimate-interest access by statute, 20 March 2026Registrar's announcement; Law 25(I)/2026, in force; by 4 October 2026 no procedure for it was found, and the Registrar's FAQ offers search only to authorities and obliged entities2022: the CJEU's WM and Sovim judgment. 2026, Finance Minister's letter: transposes art. 74 of Directive (EU) 2024/1640, aimed at compliance with that judgment, after a Commission letter of formal notice.603604605Low · company · neutralChanges who may see owners' details in the register; no filing is added or removed for the company.
Cash payments capped at €10,000, 31 December 2024Law 172(I)/2024; in forceDISY MP Dimitris Dimitriou's bill: the EU limit due in 2027 should apply 'as soon as possible' to curb suspicious transactions and money laundering (committee report). Per the Cyprus News Agency, the President referred it back over 'in application of' a Regulation applying from 10 July 2027; re-passed 23 December 2024.606607608Medium · own name, company · adds burdenA limit for every business selling goods or services, but it bites only on cash payments over €10,000, single or linked; breach is a crime.
Casino customers exempted from the cash cap, April 2025Law 35(I)/2025; no end datePreamble, written in at the Legal Service's request: the cap put the licensed casino at a competitive disadvantage to casinos outside the Republic's control and hit it disproportionately, as its business uses very much cash; the Ministry of Finance would accept it only until 10 July 2027.609610Low · company · easesExempts only casino customers' payments from the cash cap; it reaches the licensed casino, not other businesses.
Banks to avoid shell companies in listed cases, 2 November 2018Central Bank circular; revoked 2019, rules absorbed into its directiveNone stated, nor in MONEYVAL's 2019 report, which lists the guidance among initiatives that 'specifically addressed the particular risks faced by Cyprus'.611612Medium · company · adds burdenReaches only companies with no physical presence or economic activity where incorporated: banks avoid them in listed cases and decide the rest on risk.

What is coming · Pending and planned

What is coming: Cyprus bills mostly undated, EU dates fixed, several transpositions late

As of 6 October 2026 the Cyprus changes that touch a founder are bills, drafts or plans, and few have a dated next step. EU acts set fixed dates; for consumer credit, the single permit, pay transparency and beneficial-owner register access the Cyprus deadline had passed with no law found. Nothing here is in force unless a row says so.

Cyprus: bills, drafts and plans

ChangeStatus on 6 October 2026Next dated stepWeight
Companies (Amendment) (No. 2) Bill of 2026: the Registrar could strike a company off, or refuse any right, filing or application under the Companies Law, where its purpose or operation tends to undermine the Republic, national security or public order or endangers state sovereignty, 'without any reasoning being required'.Government bill tabled 17 September 2026, in the Energy, Commerce, Industry and Tourism Committee; pending.None dated; the Nomoplatform record shows only tabling and referral, and the House's weekly committee programme could not be read.596597627628Medium · company · adds burdenA new power to strike off or refuse filings without reasons, but only where a company's purpose or operation tends to undermine the Republic, national security or public order.

Source trap

A 'pending' label does not prove a bill is alive. Under rule 84B of the House's Rules of Procedure (unofficial codification of January 2020; later amendments of the Rules not checked), members' bills pending in committee at the end of a term whose discussion had not begun, and those of MPs not re-elected, are deemed withdrawn when the new term starts; the rule names only members' bills. A 2025 member's bill fixing sound licences at three years is still shown pending on Nomoplatform but has probably lapsed.359360

EU acts already adopted, with dates that reach Cyprus

ActWhat changes and whenCyprus on 6 October 2026Weight
Anti-money-laundering (AML) Regulation (EU) 2024/1624.Applies 10 July 2027: EUR 10,000 cash cap; beneficial-owner changes reported within 28 days; nominees disclose their status.Same cash cap since 31 December 2024; changes now filed within 45 days; no adjusting act in CyLaw's 2026 index.652653654655Medium · company, foreign owner · adds burdenThe cash cap stays at €10,000; among the changes for a company are a 28-day window for beneficial-owner changes (now 45 days) and nominees disclosing their status.
AML Directive (EU) 2024/1640.Register-access articles due 10 July 2026; the rest mostly 10 July 2027.Legitimate-interest access enacted by Law 25(I)/2026 (20 March 2026), but no implementing directive or procedure found by 4 October 2026; register-access rules only a draft, 'under processing'.604656657Low · company · neutralChanges who may see beneficial-owner details and how; the draft lets register keepers set access rules, and the records show no new filing for the company.
The EU anti-money-laundering authority's (AMLA) standards on customer due diligence.Final draft (30 September 2026) awaits Commission adoption; as drafted, applies six months after entry into force.As drafted, applies to all obliged entities.658Medium · own name, company, foreign owner · direction unclearSets how every bank and other obliged entity identifies a customer, so it reaches every founder who opens an account; the records do not compare it with today's practice.
VAT in the digital age, Directive (EU) 2025/516.Article 2 from 1 January 2027: the EUR 10,000 threshold counts only goods dispatched from home; union one-stop-shop sellers deemed to opt for destination VAT. Platform rules from 1 July 2028 (one may apply as late as 1 January 2030).Draft 'under processing'; no tabled bill found in the Gazette or CyLaw.659660661Medium · own name, company · direction unclearReaches only sellers to consumers in other EU states: the €10,000 threshold is limited to goods dispatched from the seller's own state, and union one-stop-shop users are deemed to opt for the buyer's state's VAT.
Council Regulation (EU) 2026/382 on low-value imports.1 July 2026-1 July 2028: EUR 3 per item on consignments up to EUR 150 imported VAT-exempt under the Import One-Stop Shop or by post.EU regulation; Commission review by 1 December 2027.662Medium · own name, company, foreign owner · adds burdenA new duty of €3 per item, but only on consignments up to €150 imported VAT-exempt under the Import One-Stop Shop or by post.
Product Liability Directive (EU) 2024/2853.Transpose by 9 December 2026; where the manufacturer is outside the EU the importer and the authorised representative answer, or failing both the fulfilment service provider; if no EU operator can be identified, a distributor that does not name one or its supplier within a month of a request answers.Bill consulted February-March 2026; no law in CyLaw's 2026 list.663664Medium · own name, company, foreign owner · adds burdenWhere the maker is outside the EU, the importer and authorised representative answer, failing both the fulfilment provider; with no EU operator, a distributor naming none on request; it reaches sellers of non-EU goods.
Consumer Credit Directive (EU) 2023/2225.Applies 20 November 2026: third-party 'buy now, pay later' in scope; a shop's own deferral with no third party offering credit, free of interest and charges except limited late-payment charges, paid within 50 days of delivery (14 for online sellers that are not small or medium-sized enterprises), stays outside.Due 20 November 2025; no law in CyLaw's lists.665Medium · own name, company · adds burdenReaches only sellers offering deferred payment: third-party buy-now-pay-later enters consumer-credit rules; a shop's own free deferral paid within 50 days (14 for online non-SMEs) stays outside.
Pay Transparency Directive (EU) 2023/970.Due 7 June 2026: pay information for job applicants, no questions on pay history, no size threshold.Draft consulted in 2025; not enacted (none in CyLaw's 2026 laws).666Medium · own name, company · adds burdenReaches any employer recruiting, with no size threshold: pay information for applicants and no questions on pay history; a hiring procedure, not a filing.
Single Permit Directive (EU) 2024/1233 (recast).Due 21 May 2026: states 'shall allow' a third-country worker to change employer; they may require notice, a labour-market check or up to six months with the first employer, and where notice is required may suspend the change for up to 45 days and oppose it.No measure found on EUR-Lex, in the Gazette search or CyLaw's 2026 laws.667Medium · own name, company, foreign owner · mixedReaches only employers of third-country workers: such a worker may change employer; states may require notice, a labour-market check or up to six months with the first employer.
Company-law Directive (EU) 2025/25.Transpose by 31 July 2027, apply 31 July 2028: EU Company Certificate, digital power of attorney, no apostille on register copies.No law found in CyLaw's 2025-2026 lists or on EUR-Lex; nothing changes for founders before 31 July 2028.528529668Medium · company, foreign owner · easesEases papers only for companies of one EU state acting in another: a Company Certificate, a digital power of attorney and register copies with no apostille.
EU Digital Identity Wallet (Regulation (EU) 2024/1183).Counted by the researchers: states provide a wallet by 24 December 2026; banks and other private relying parties bound to strong authentication, except micro and small firms, accept it on request by 24 December 2027.Wallet tender closes 13 October 2026; whether a 2026 implementing act moves the date is not said in texts read.669670Low · own name, company · easesAdds a way to identify, not a duty: banks and other relying parties bound to strong authentication must accept the wallet on request; micro and small firms need not accept it.
Packaging Regulation (EU) 2025/40.Applies 12 August 2026: producers register in each state where they make packaging available and may not sell unregistered (a producer responsibility organisation they entrust registers unless the state says otherwise); national registers follow within 18 months of an implementing act due by 12 February 2026.No register found (Department of Environment, Green Dot); the Commission's draft act closed for comments on 10 September 2026 and was not adopted on 6 October 2026.274275Medium · own name, company · adds burdenAdds a registration, but only for producers: those who first make packaging or packaged goods available in a state or sell directly to end users in another.
Source trap

The EUR 3 duty is not per article. Per the Commission's guidance of 2 June 2026, which says it is explanatory and not legally binding, an item is goods in a consignment sharing tariff classification, description and, where given, origin, and because of IT limits the duty applies per declaration line irrespective of the quantity on it, provided the declaration is worth up to EUR 150 in total.662671

Calendar · Dates to follow

Calendar to 2028: fixed deadlines of adopted acts and meetings where change may be announced

Each entry says whether it is a date set by an adopted act, a meeting at which something may be announced, a forecast or an expectation. Dates marked 'counted' are the researchers' arithmetic from the act. Entries before 6 October 2026 are the recent dates the guide relies on.

2026

1 Jan 2026
Tax
2026 tax reform applies Date already passed, set by the 2025 tax laws: corporate tax 15%; defence contribution on dividends 5% (17% kept on pre-2026 profits paid by 2031); no deemed distribution of 2026 profits; State stamp duty repealed; a company registers for tax within 60 days of incorporation.4389575583682High · company · mixedEvery company meets the corporate tax rate, up from 12.5% to 15%; defence contribution on dividends falls from 17% to 5%, 17% kept on pre-2026 profits paid by 2031; State stamp duty and 2026 deemed distribution go.
6 Apr 2026
Residence, work, pensions
Training levy for the self-employed starts Date already passed, set by Law 15(I)/2026 and K.D.P. 128/2026 (K.D.P.: the Cyprus numbering of regulatory administrative acts): the self-employed pay the 0.5% Human Resource Development levy, collected by the Social Insurance Services, whose 2026 table still says 'from 5.1.2026'.145High · own name · adds burdenA new recurring payment every self-employed person meets: 0.5% of insurable earnings, collected by the Social Insurance Services; registration with them counts as registration for the levy.
1 Jul 2026
Tax
Company tax registration only through CY Login Date, now passed, of a rule the Tax Department announced on 9 April 2026: a company, overseas company or registered partnership files its Tax Register request only after logging into Tax For All through CY Login; requests made otherwise are rejected.43225240540Medium · company · adds burdenChanges the procedure of a step every company takes within 60 days of incorporation: its Tax Register request is accepted only after logging into Tax For All through CY Login; other requests are rejected.

Open questions: what the round did not establish, where it looked, and when to check again

Forty-four questions a founder would act on, each with what the records do establish as of 6 October 2026, where the answer was looked for, what would close it and a recheck date. They are grouped by the guide section they belong to.

Look again bySectionQuestionEstablished so farWould close it
23 Oct 2026PremisesThe 2026 leisure-premises law
What does the law passed on 6 April 2026 say, why was it referred, and when will the court give its opinion?		Only the bill as tabled has been read; the House committee amended it, and per press the passed text changes opening hours. Per press, the President referred it to the court after the Legal Service found that 'a number of its provisions' conflict with the Constitution; neither source names them. The Supreme Constitutional Court lists references by number only, so which one is this law is not established, and no date for an opinion is published.367368370371The reference document or a Presidency announcement naming its number; the court's opinion; the passed text from the House.Looked: Nomoplatform (tabled text); parliament.cy (security check); the court's cause lists for September and October 2026 and its list of decided references; gov.cy; press of April-June 2026.
31 Oct 2026AheadCommittee dates of pending bills
When will House committees take up the land-purchase bills, the national-security Companies bill and the pension bill?		No committee date was found for any of them by 6 October 2026. The Interior Committee chair said it would return to the land bills in October 2026; the Companies bill's record shows only tabling and referral on 17 September 2026; the pension bill had not been tabled. The House's site answered scripted requests with a security check, and the only 2026 web-archive copy of its weekly committee programme is of 6 August 2026.553628630715The House's weekly committee programme read in a browser, or a later archive capture.Looked: parliament.cy (security check); web-archive captures of parliament.cy pages, September-October 2026; Nomoplatform records and news.
14 Dec 2026AheadPayments package dates
From when would PSD3 and the Payment Services Regulation apply?		Neither is adopted: provisional agreement on 27 November 2025, COREPER confirmation on 22 April 2026 and approval of the agreed text by the Parliament's ECON committee on 5 May 2026; both files read 'Awaiting Council's 1st reading position', indicative plenary date 14 December 2026. The application dates are not in the documents read.674675The agreed texts, or publication in the Official Journal.Looked: The Parliament's procedure files 2023/0209(COD) and 2023/0210(COD); the Council's release of 27 November 2025. The agreed texts approved on 5 May 2026 were not read.
24 Dec 2026AheadEU wallet deadline
Does Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1731 move the 24 December 2026 date by which each state must provide an EU Digital Identity Wallet?		The 24 December 2026 date is counted by the researchers: 24 months from the entry into force of the 2024 implementing acts on 24 December 2024. Implementing Regulation 2026/1731, adopted under art. 5a(23) of the eIDAS Regulation and in force since 11 August 2026, amends those acts; no text read on 6 October 2026 says whether it moves the date.669670A Commission statement, or the launch of Cyprus's wallet.Looked: Regulation (EU) 2024/1183; the 2024 implementing acts; Implementing Regulation (EU) 2026/1731.
31 Dec 2026AheadStartup Visa after 2026
Will the Startup Visa continue after December 2026, and on what terms?		The Practical Guide, dated December 2024 (Word edition last modified 18 August 2026), says 'The Scheme is valid until December 2026 and up to 150 visas can be issued'. No decision on the scheme after December 2026 had been published by 6 October 2026, and the Deputy Ministry's news list had no item on it from 11 September. Per press, the Deputy Ministry appears before the House Finance Committee for its 2027 budget hearing on 6 November 2026.197639646A Council of Ministers decision, a Deputy Ministry announcement, or a new edition of the guide.Looked: The gov.cy service page 'Apply for a Cyprus Startup Visa'; the Practical Guide (PDF and Word); the Deputy Ministry's news list (6 October 2026).
31 Dec 2026CompanyFirst beneficial-owner confirmation
When does a company that first files its beneficial owners in January 2027 make its first yearly confirmation?		Per the Registrar's notice of 16 September 2026, a company whose 90-day deadline for the first filing falls in the window of 1 October-31 December 2026, or which files within it, also confirms by 31 December 2026. The notice is silent on a company first filing in January 2027; a first confirmation in late 2027 is an inference.44251A Registrar notice or FAQ on companies incorporated in the last quarter of a year.Looked: The Registrar's notice of 16 September 2026; K.D.P. 112/2021 as amended.
31 Dec 2026CompanyA new company's provisional tax
Is the 31 December 2026 provisional-tax date extended, and what does a new company that expects no chargeable income file? 6 Apr 2027Who to hireOwn filings and 'practising law'
Does a company's own officer who prepares its filings 'practise law' under the Advocates Law?		The Advocates Law defines practising law to include drafting any application, statement or resolution on the formation, registration, organisation or dissolution of a legal person, and opens it only to licensed advocates (Cap. 2, ss. 2(1), 11). 6 Apr 2027LadderFounder's unemployment benefit
Can a founder employed by his own company draw unemployment benefit while the company still exists?		Self-employed periods do not count towards unemployment benefit (Social Insurance Law 59(I)/2010, s. 24(2)(α)); a shareholder working in his own private company is insured as employed (First Schedule, Part I, para. 5). A day counts as unemployment only if the claimant proves he is unemployed, capable of and available for work (or in approved training); a day of gainful occupation does not count unless he could normally do it besides his usual work, outside its hours, and earns no more than a prescribed amount that day (s. 31(4)).6383Social Insurance Services guidance or decisions, or court judgments on owner-employees.Looked: Social Insurance Law 59(I)/2010, ss. 24 and 31 and the First Schedule (consolidated text, 6 October 2026); the Social Insurance Services' guides for insured persons and employers.

Method · How this page was built

Memory first, then the documents, every fact attacked, every item checked

The guide is a view of memory records. Each record is one checked statement with its source, and the guide says nothing that a record does not carry.

What adversarial checking did to the 385 researcher facts

Sixteen research slices in three waves; each slice read again by two verifiers whose task was to refute it.

Confirmed as written — 104 (27.0%) Corrected: qualifier, status, date, act or scope — 277 (71.9%) Refuted — 4 (1.0%)

The verifiers also added 94 facts of their own; six facts were dropped by decision. With three corrected editions of earlier records that makes 472 records stored.

What the checkers did to the 548 items of this page

Seven writers wrote the sections from the records only; seven checkers compared every item with the records it cites.

Confirmed as written — 331 (60.4%) Corrected — 198 (36.1%) Dropped — 19 (3.5%)

The checkers also added 30 items from records the writers had not used.

Order of work. Memory was asked first: ten narrow questions, three answered (company steps, the online shop, café licences), five in part (the self-employed, the lawyer's role, upkeep prices, the foreign element, what is coming), two not at all (reasons for reforms, real cases). The web part then ran in three waves. The first covered the ladder, cases, whom to hire, the foreign element, reasons and what is coming; the second the order of steps and times, premises costs, a second pass on cases and hiring, and what each rung gives; the third the registrations of a seller of goods, founders' own accounts, and the practical points of the barest path: bank accounts, CY Login and the advocates' stamp.

What the verifiers corrected. Few facts were wrong outright; most lost a condition on the way. The common faults were a qualifier dropped while shortening, an inference stated as a finding, an official guide used without checking that it predates a rule change, and a negative wider than the search behind it. One refuted claim, that the World Bank publishes no per-step figures for Cyprus, led to the per-step data now in the cases section.

From records to page. After each batch the stored text, tags and source of every record were compared with the prepared files byte by byte: 472 of 472 identical. Five earlier records were superseded because this round showed them wrong or incomplete in a way that misleads; three of them were replaced by corrected editions. The checkers of this page found the same kind of error as the verifiers: compression that drops a qualifier, widens a negative or sets an order of steps that no document sets.

Records stored once. Since the revision of 7 October 2026 the appendix lists each cited record by the publisher of the document it was read in, the date it was read and a link to that document. The record itself, with its full text, its source and the pages that cite it, is kept once, on the shared Cyprus records page, which both Cyprus pages link to; a reference number shows the record's text on hover. A page is a view over memory, not a second copy of it. Links inside the text go only to documents that a cited record was read in.

Weights. Rows that describe a change carry the page's own rating of how much it matters to someone opening a business, as of 6 October 2026. These are past reforms, pending bills, calendar events and the history of the investor criteria. High: it opens or closes a rung or path; adds or removes a registration, a licence, or a recurring filing or payment that every business on that rung meets; or changes a tax rate or threshold most of them meet. Medium: it changes an amount, deadline, form or procedure of an existing duty, or reaches only part of a rung. Low: wording, consolidation, a technical change, or one that very few founders meet. Unknown: no text is published, or the effect turns on figures not yet set. Each weight names whom it touches (own name, company, foreign owner) and whether it adds burden or eases; the line under it gives the reason from the row's records. A weight is a judgment, not a measurement. The row itself states the status (announced, adopted, in force) and the source; the weight does not repeat them. The rubric takes the selection factors of the European Commission's Better Regulation toolbox (magnitude, relative size for small firms, sign and intensity) and the explicit 'unknown' of the UK Regulatory Initiatives Grid.

Limits. This is a reference study, not legal, immigration or tax advice. Firms' prices are their own published prices on the dates given, and packages include different things; times are what an authority, a provider or a firm publishes; nothing is added up into a total cost or time. Cases rest on public registers, the businesses' own pages and press or founders' accounts; no public source gave the issue date of any café's licence. Greek-language acts were read in Greek, and quotes from them are this study's rendering.
